### 39) [2611.03961](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03961v1): Mass-Metallicity Relation of the Fossil Disk in Six Nearby Galaxies using JWST RGB Stars

<p> Avinash Ck, Divakara Mayya, Alessandro Bressan, Jairo Andres Alzate Trujillo, Léo Girardi, Shaunak Mishra, Bolivia Cuevas-Otahola </p>
<p>
We here present the first ever mass-metallicity relation (MZR) of fossil disk populations using the resolved stellar populations in the James Webb Space Telescope Near-Infrared Camera (JWST/NIRCam) images of six nearby galaxies. Both the mass and metallicity were determined using the Red Giant Branch (RGB) stars, employing methods that have been well established to study the halo populations of nearby galaxies using the Hubble Space Telescope images. Specifically, we obtained the mass and metallicity by comparing the color, magnitude and number density near the tip of the RGB (TRGB) stars in the F115W-F200W vs F200W color-magnitude diagrams (CMDs) with that of the PARSEC isochrones of 4 and 13~Gyr. The RGB metallicities of the disks of nearby galaxies closely match those found in galaxies at redshifts z&gt;8, with the metallicity range following a power-law relation with the fossil disk mass. The power-law index of the fossil disk MZR, γ≈0.26, compares well with the typical value found in MZRs obtained using gas metallicities across redshifts. Through this work, we illustrate the utility of JWST/NIRCam images for obtaining the mass and metallicity of the fossil disks of nearby galaxies at precisions that were not possible earlier, which opens up a new method to carry out the near-field cosmological studies.
</p>

### 40) [2611.03819](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03819v1): Towards Self-consistent Modeling of Quasi-Stars and their NLTE Spectra: Implications for Little Red Dots

<p> Jake B. Hassan, Rosalba Perna, Mitchell C. Begelman, Yan-Fei Jiang, Matteo Cantiello, Philip J. Armitage, Taeho Ryu </p>
<p>
Quasi-stars, massive envelopes surrounding rapidly accreting black hole (BH) seeds, are promising candidates for explaining the rapid formation of supermassive black holes in the early Universe and may account for some of the JWST-discovered Little Red Dots (LRDs). We present a self-consistent framework combining stellar evolution calculations with non-local thermodynamic equilibrium (NLTE) spectral synthesis to test this connection. Our models drop the polytropic assumptions of previous work: the envelope is described entirely by mixing-length theory, with saturated convection and radiative energy setting the luminosity at an inner boundary a few hundred Schwarzschild radii from the BH. This removes the BH-mass limits of earlier models, and we follow the evolution up to BH-to-total mass ratios approaching unity. We then use TLUSTY to compute the first converged NLTE atmospheric models for the surface conditions and primordial hydrogen-helium composition expected in quasi-stars. Our models produce cool photospheres, prominent Balmer breaks, and Balmer and Paschen absorption features. NLTE effects substantially modify line strengths, increasing Balmer absorption depths by ~ 35-55&percnt; relative to LTE models, while having a minor effect on the continuum. Comparison with observed LRD spectral energy distributions shows broad agreement in continuum shape, although our models do not reproduce their commonly observed strong emission lines. Under the quasi-star interpretation, comparisons with individual LRDs suggest characteristic quasi-star masses of approximately 10^5-10^7 M<sub>&odot;</sub>. These results establish a physically motivated connection between quasi-star evolution and observable spectra, providing a framework for testing quasi-stars as progenitors of LRDs while identifying limitations of current atmospheric models.
</p>

### 41) [2611.03801](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03801v1): Lyα in Compact Broad-Line Emitters, Including Little Red Dots, Is More Closely Linked to the Host Galaxy than to the Broad-Line Nucleus

<p> Yongda Zhu, Zheng Ma, Xiaojing Lin, Junyu Zhang, Jianwei Lyu, Xiaohui Fan, Eiichi Egami, Andrew J. Bunker, Sijia Cai, Laura C. Keating, Joseph Shields, Zhiyuan Ji, Xiangyu Jin, Weizhe Liu, Suprabhas Narisetty, George H. Rieke, Marcia J. Rieke, Pierluigi Rinaldi, Fengwu Sun </p>
<p>
JWST has found many faint broad-line AGN at z&gt;4, but it is unclear whether the Lyα we see from them comes from the accreting black hole or from its host galaxy. The answer matters for how these sources are interpreted and for how many ionizing photons they release. We use deep JWST/NIRSpec spectroscopy from the DIVER program to measure Lyα in 12 compact broad-line emitters (CBEs) at z=4.2&ndash;5.5 in GOODS-N. CBEs include little red dots (LRDs), little blue dots, and other broad-line AGN. Six are detected in Lyα. Every source shows a broad-line region, yet Lyα does not follow it: the sources with the strongest Hα relative to the UV include weak detections and nondetections, and every detected Lyα line (FWHM ≈300&ndash;1000~km~s<sup>-1</sup>) is narrower than broad Hα (1100&ndash;3300~km~s<sup>-1</sup>). Instead, Lyα appears to track the host galaxy. We model the AGN-subtracted host with a dust-attenuated SED fit. Including the upper limits, the observed Lyα follows the Lyα production in this host model more closely than it follows Hα/UV, in both equivalent width and luminosity. Every detection exceeds its host-model value by a factor of 1.5&ndash;18, leaving room for a nuclear contribution. The black hole is visible in these sources, but the Lyα we observe is more closely tied to its host. The median effective Lyα escape fraction of the detections is only 0.09, which suggests that ionizing escape from CBEs is typically modest and varies widely.
</p>

### 42) [2611.03799](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03799v1): Tiny Galaxies, Mighty Nitrogen: Linking Chemical Anomalies to Compact Galaxies at High Redshift using JWST/JADES

<p> Urvi Thakurdesai, Xihan Ji, Yuki Isobe, Roberto Maiolino, Vasily Belokurov, Courtney Carreira, Brant Robertson, Will McClymont, David Puskas, Andrew Bunker, Stephane Charlot, Mirko Curti, Qiao Duan, Francesco D'Eugenio, Maria Koller, Jonah Powley, Pierluigi Rinaldi, Jan Scholtz, Charlotte Simmonds, Sandro Tacchella, Hannah Ubler </p>
<p>
The origin of anomalous chemical enrichment in high-redshift galaxies discovered by JWST remains a key question in galaxy evolution. Enhanced nitrogen abundances have been identified in several galaxies at early cosmic times, but the physical conditions driving this enrichment, and their connection to galaxy morphology, remain unclear. We test whether morphologically compact galaxies at 4 &lt; z &lt; 7 show stronger chemical anomalies than extended systems. Using JWST/NIRSpec spectroscopic data, we construct samples of compact and extended galaxies and analyse their rest-frame spectra. We stack the spectra of each population and measure key UV and optical emission lines. We detect nitrogen emission in the compact galaxy stack, including NIV], and [NII], while also detecting key carbon and oxygen lines. The detection of [OIII]λ4363 enables a direct-Te determination of the oxygen abundance. From these measurements, we infer chemical abundance ratios, including log(N/O) and log(C/O). We find that compact galaxies exhibit elevated log(N/O) (by ~ 0.7 dex) and log(C/O) (by ~ 0.5 dex) abundance ratios relative to extended systems. The abundance patterns of compact galaxy stacks are comparable to those of previously identified individual nitrogen-enhanced galaxies at high redshift and second-generation globular-cluster stars in the Milky Way. These results suggest a possible link between galaxy compactness and chemical enrichment pathways in the early Universe. Deeper JWST NIRSpec IFU observations of compact high-redshift galaxies will be crucial for determining the origin, spatial distribution, and evolutionary significance of these abundance patterns.
</p>

### 43) [2611.03798](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03798v1): Surface Nitrogen enrichment from the LMC to the Early Universe: the roles of rotation, overshooting and mass loss

<p> Gautham N. Sabhahit, Jorick. S Vink </p>
<p>
The strong nitrogen (N) features observed in high-redshift galaxies, such as GN-z11, with JWST point to an efficient source of N production in the early Universe. Very massive stars (VMSs) are promising candidates owing to their strong winds and large convective cores of CNO-processed material. Whether massive-star surfaces in general become N-enriched depends on the interplay of convective-boundary mixing (overshooting), rotation, and mass loss, which remains poorly constrained. Using a grid of MESA evolutionary models, we isolate the overshooting and rotation channels at 40, 100, and 200 M<sub>&odot;</sub> and investigate how these channels bring N-rich material to the surface of massive and very massive stars as a function of initial mass and metallicity. We also construct a synthetic Large Magellanic Cloud (LMC) population based on a larger initial mass grid ranging from 20-300 M<sub>&odot;</sub> for comparison with the observed N-enriched fraction. VMS surface enrichment is remarkably robust across cosmic metallicity. At lower metallicities, wind stripping is weaker and the overshooting channel shuts off entirely, yet a modest amount of rotation of ~90 km s<sup>-1</sup> alone still enriches the surface. At higher metallicity, stronger winds dominate and uncover the core almost independently of the adopted mixing. The two channels operate through physically distinct mechanisms: overshooting enriches the surface only once winds strip the envelope down into the N-rich layers left by the receding core, giving an abrupt jump. Rotation, by contrast, mixes N outward continuously through the layers above the core, producing a gradual rise. Applied to the LMC, a population with modest rotation drawn from the observed velocity distribution and modest overshooting reproduces the observed N-enriched fraction without large core boundary mixing efficiencies otherwise required by non-rotating models.
</p>

### 44) [2611.03790](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03790v2): How bursty star formation shapes spectral diversity at high redshifts: breaking degeneracies in early galaxy formation

<p> Viola Gelli, Charlotte A. Mason, Daniel P. Stark, Mengtao Tang, Julian B. Muñoz, Zuyi Chen, Ivan Nikolić </p>
<p>
JWST has revealed an unexpected abundance of z&gt;10 galaxies and striking spectral diversity. Here, we model bursty star formation histories (SFHs) to explore how they shape this diversity and whether spectroscopy can discriminate between explanations for the UV luminosity function (UVLF) excess. We find strong burstiness (σ_UV(M_h=10^10M<sub>&odot;</sub>)~0.9 mag), increasing toward low halo masses, is required to reproduce the z~6 UVLF and observed fractions of galaxies in recent star formation upturns and downturns, traced by SFR_3/SFR_50. This burstiness naturally reproduces UVLFs and SFR upturn and downturn fractions over z~6-11. With increasing redshift, we predict galaxies in recent upturns (SFR_3/SFR_50&gt;5) increasingly dominate the UVLF bright end, reflecting rising accretion rates and burstier low-mass halos. This results in higher median emission-line equivalent widths (EWs), bluer UV slopes and weaker Balmer breaks at fixed M_UV, while distributions of these properties broaden. We find the downturn fraction (SFR_3/SFR_50&lt;0.2) is particularly sensitive to stochasticity: increasing towards faint M_UV if burstiness increases in lower mass halos, and evolving with redshift only if stochasticity does. However, the model underpredicts the z~14 UVLF, so we explore two models which match the UVLF: increased burstiness and increased star formation efficiency. From z=10 to 14, at fixed M_UV, increased burstiness predicts the downturn fraction increases by ~2x and the weak-emitter fraction (Hβ EW&lt;50Å) by ~1.5x. By contrast, the increased efficiency model predicts a constant downturn fraction and declining weak-emitter fraction. Overall, our results indicate burstiness is important for explaining spectral diversity and evolution at z~6-14, and that spectroscopic samples spanning a broad M_UV range can establish its role in the z~14 UVLF excess.
</p>

### 45) [2611.03786](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03786v1): 3D dark matter map of the JWST COSMOS-Web field using diffusion-based generative priors

<p> Diana Scognamiglio, Brandon Zhao, Katherine L. Bouman, Olivier Doré, Caitlin M. Casey, Maximilien Franco, Ghassem Gozaliasl, David Harvey, Olivier Ilbert, Jeffrey B. Jewell, Jeyhan S. Kartaltepe, Anton M. Koekemoer, Gavin Leroy, Richard Massey, Jason Rhodes, Brant E. Robertson, Marko Shuntov, Sune Toft, Greta Toni, Michael A. Troxel </p>
<p>
Mapping the three-dimensional distribution of dark matter is key to tracing the growth of cosmic structure. Two-dimensional weak lensing mass maps recover its projected distribution, but they blend structures at different distances along the line of sight. We reconstruct the three-dimensional matter distribution in the 0.54 deg<sup>2</sup> COSMOS-Web field using deep James Webb Space Telescope imaging, with an effective source density of 121 galaxies arcmin^-2 We combine a physical weak lensing forward model with a diffusion-based Bayesian generative prior learned from cosmological simulations, yielding a posterior distribution of the matter field across 20 lens planes to z ≃ 2, doubling the redshift reach of previous Hubble Space Telescope-based 3D reconstructions and approaching cosmic noon, an epoch of rapid galaxy growth. The reconstruction separates structures that overlap in projection and recovers non-Gaussian matter structure across a range of spatial scales, including over-densities associated with independently identified galaxy clusters that were not detected in earlier weak lensing maps. Posterior sampling quantifies the range of three-dimensional matter distributions allowed by the data, enabling a probabilistic visualization of the cosmic web while propagating uncertainties in its inferred structure. Null and simulation-based tests show that the recovered structures are supported by the lensing signal and are consistent with expectations from the fiducial ΛCDM cosmology. These results extend three-dimensional weak lensing mapping into the high-redshift, non-Gaussian regime and establish a route towards probabilistic mapping of the evolving cosmic web with current wide-field lensing surveys.
</p>

### 46) [2611.02915](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02915v1): An Empirical Temperature Relation for Chemical Abundances in AGN

<p> Sophia R. Flury, Oli L. Dors, Rogemar A. Riffel, Karla Z. Arellano-Córdova, Mark Armah, Guillermo F. Hägele, Mónica V. Cardaci, Igor A. Zinchenko </p>
<p>
We present for the very first time the empirical temperature structure of the narrow line region (NLR) clouds of active galactic nuclei (AGNs) based on a sample of [N II] and [O III] auroral line measurements. The scaling of Te([N II]) with Te([O III]), expressed as the t2-t3 relation, is key to deriving accurate chemical abundances, in AGNs and other line-emitting nebulae, particularly when only one auroral line is observed. Here, we provide the first robust empirical description of t2-t3 for AGNs: t2(10^4 K)=1.285*t3(10^4 K)+0.799. We find substantial changes in 12+log(O/H) when compared to other t2-t3 calibrations when using the direct, or Te, method with only the [O III] 4363 auroral line. We find that adopting our t2-t3 most accurately recovers the fully direct method 12+log(O/H). To interpret the physical mechanisms behind the AGN t2-t3, we compare the observed distribution of [N II] 6584/5755 and [O III] 5007/4363 flux ratios to a variety of ionization model predictions. We find evidence for power law density gradients, pressure stratification, and (sometimes significant) contributions from astrophysical shocks driving much of the dispersion on these flux ratios while metal cooling dominates the primary shape of the distribution. With more accurate t2-t3 and 12+log(O/H), the properties of the interstellar medium photoionized by AGN can be better characterized and understood across a wide range of cosmic time and may bring better context for novel observations at z&gt;6 with JWST, with implications for understanding chemical enrichment in the earliest stages of galaxy evolution.
</p>

### 47) [2611.01595](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01595v1): Physically Motivated Stellar Populations for Galaxies at Cosmic Dawn with a Temperature-Dependent IMF

<p> Gibson B. Bowling, Charles L. Steinhardt, Lachlan J. Hobart, Carter Meyerhoff, Jackson Headon, Christopher Cain, Timothy Carleton, Kevin S. Croker, Hunter D. Sparks, Maria J. Sanchez Rincon, Maksymilian T. Malinski, Zachary Borowiak, Caleb S. Watson, Rafael Ortiz, Rogier A. Windhorst, Rolf A. Jansen, Seth H. Cohen, Tanish Parida </p>
<p>
The first few years of James Webb Space Telescope observations have identified previously undetected galaxies at z&gt;12 and provided new constraints on galaxies at z&gt;8. Many of these objects have stellar masses that are difficult to reconcile with ΛCDM, inferred from models not calibrated for the high-redshift Universe. This necessitates new, more astrophysically plausible stellar population models for estimating stellar masses of galaxies at cosmic dawn. We introduce a simple stellar population (SSP) library, Simple Stellar Populations with Temperature (SSPT), computed using a temperature-dependent stellar initial mass function and its implementation in the public code CIGALE. When used in a configuration designed for high-redshift galaxies that requires young stellar population ages, the new models predict stellar masses that are ~0.3-0.5 dex lower than estimates from similar CIGALE analyses using models calibrated to the local Universe, and nearly 2 dex lower than estimates from analyses using other codes and softer constraints on stellar ages. Application of these physically motivated stellar populations suggests that stellar masses of early star-forming galaxies are not in tension with ΛCDM, and that galaxy formation from virialized halos remains a reasonable hypothesis at the highest redshifts. The SSPT SSPs and their CIGALE implementation are released publicly.
</p>

### 48) [2611.00215](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00215v2): A VLT/CRIRES+ study of helium 1.083μm absorption features towards the inner Orion Nebula

<p> Thomas J. Haworth, Mark McCaughrean, Mario van den Ancker, James E. Owen, John Bally, Samuel Pearson </p>
<p>
JWST NIRCam F115W observations towards the inner Orion Nebula revealed a diverse array of previously unknown dark features. The most likely cause was absorption or suppressed emission of the helium 1.083μm triplet which lies in that filter. We have obtained VLT/CRIRES+ high-resolution spectrographic observations over a selection of different dark targets, confirming that the features are indeed explained by a reduction in the flux of the helium 1.083μm triplet, a reduction that is fairly uniform across the line. The Orion Nebula is a blister HII region, with a dense, bright ionised layer on the far side from the observer near the Orion A molecular cloud and lower density gas elsewhere. Using a simple radiative transfer model, we show that dark features arise when emission from the dense layer is absorbed by intermediate density helium (e.g. in clumps, globules, or winds from objects like proplyds) on the near side of the dense layer.
</p>

Willott, Guillaume Desprez, Vesna Pirc Jevšenak, Yoshihisa Asada, Gregor Rihtaršič, Lilan Yang, Roberto Abraham, Gabriel B. Brammer, Viicente Estrada-Carpenter, Jasleen Matharu, Lamiya A. Little red dots (LRDs) are an intriguing new class of sources in the early universe discovered by the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) characterized by V-shaped spectra and extremely compact sizes. Their underlying mechanisms still remain the subject of ongoing debate. We present JWST observations of a gravitationally lensed LRD nicknamed "Best Friend" at z<sub>spec</sub> = 8.297±0.002. Its high magnification factor of μ= 36^+8_-12 makes it the highest magnified LRD reported to date and unveils both the resolved structure of its host and a central engine less than 7 pc in size. The spectrum of the central engine, in which broad lines are not detected, possibly owing to the limited S/N and spectral resolution, is better interpreted as black hole activity rather than a dense star cluster. The latter cannot be fully excluded, but if true, the resulting lower limit on stellar density would be &gt;1.25^+0.4_-0.3\cdot10^6 M<sub>&odot;</sub>/pc^2, which is around 2 orders of magnitude higher than stellar densities of typical clusters at high-z. By performing a spatially resolved analysis of NIRCam photometry and NIRSpec/PRISM spectroscopy, we find that the host stellar mass is log(M<sub>&sext;</sub>/M<sub>&odot;</sub>) = 7.3^+0.2_-0.8. Best Friend demonstrates the power of lensing to reveal the morphologies of LRDs.

### 50) [2611.00199](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00199v1): MARTA: A "little" black hole hidden in a clumpy, metal-poor galaxy at z~3.8

<p> Mirko Curti, Bianca Moreschini, Elisa Cataldi, Francesco Belfiore, Ivan Delvecchio, Alessandro Marconi, Filippo Mannucci, Elena Bertola, Giovanni Cresci, Anna Feltre, Roberto Maiolino, Francesca Pozzi, Letizia Scaloni, Alberto Traina, Caterina Bracci, Stefano Carniani, Carlotta Gruppioni, Giacomo Venturi, Magda Arnaboldi, Meriem Behiri, Michele Ginolfi, Mahsa Kohandel, Livia Vallini </p>
<p>
JWST has extended the search for accreting black holes into the high-redshift, low-mass regime. We report a low-mass black hole candidate in MARTA 3887, a clumpy, metal-poor galaxy at z=3.7694, combining deep JWST/NIRSpec spectroscopy, HST and JWST imaging, and archival VLT/MUSE data. We detect a broad Hα component with L_Hα,b=2.35×10^41 erg s<sup>-1</sup> and FWHM =1270^+260_-250 km s<sup>-1</sup>, with no [O III] counterpart of comparable width. Under a broad-line-region interpretation, virial calibrations give log(M_BH/M<sub>&odot;</sub>)=6.52^+0.18_-0.23 and λ_Edd=0.07, or up to 0.38 for alternative calibrations. The host comprises at least three compact clumps, with log(M<sub>&sext;</sub>/M<sub>&odot;</sub>)=8.16^+0.09_-0.10. If stellar photoionization dominates the narrow lines, the gas is highly ionized and metal poor, with 12+log(O/H)=7.68±0.04 (≃0.10 Z<sub>&odot;</sub>). The inferred M_BH/M<sub>&sext;</sub>=2.3&percnt; lies about 2 dex above the extrapolated local relation, suggesting that black-hole growth has outpaced the assembly of the observed host. If growth began only when the first 10% of the observed stellar mass had formed (t_10=92 Myr ago), a 10^2 M<sub>&odot;</sub> seed would require a lifetime-mean Eddington ratio ≃5.7; earlier growth or a more massive, e.g. direct-collapse, seed would allow sub-Eddington mean accretion. MUSE reveals extended Lyα emission (≃6.5-6.9 proper kpc), offset by +64^+10_-8 km s<sup>-1</sup> from systemic, with a provisional escape fraction of ≃0.44. Together with a Mg II doublet ratio close to the optically thin limit and a high [O III]/[O II] ratio, this makes MARTA 3887 a laboratory to study black-hole growth, stellar assembly, and ionizing-photon escape jointly.

However, no LRD had previously been spectroscopically confirmed at z&gt;10, leaving this earliest phase observationally unexplored. Using JWST/NIRSpec spectroscopy from the EMBER program, we measure a spectroscopic redshift of z<sub>spec</sub>=10.361^+0.003_-0.002 for \target, the highest-redshift LRD candidate known to date. The redshift is constrained by a prominent Lyα break and multiple tentative emission lines, including C \sc iv λ&lambda;1549,1551 and [OIII] &lambda;4363 detected at S/N=3.9 and 3.6, respectively. Its extreme NIRCam-MIRI color of m_F444W-m_F770W=1.82±0.1, characteristic V-shaped continuum with a turnover near the Balmer limit, and continuum slopes of β_UV=-2.26^+0.11_-0.11 and β_opt=1.17^+0.74_-0.70 all support its LRD nature. A possible iron complex at λ_rest~0.24&ndash;0.25 μm and a possibly high log≤ft([O III] &lambda;4363/Hγ)=0.17^+0.24_-0.22 further support an AGN-like interpretation, as seen in previously known LRDs. Tentative detection of Lyα emission may indicate enhanced Lyα transmission at z~10. Our findings extend the observational frontier of BH growth into the seed-BH formation era, and upcoming MIRI spectroscopy will further establish the physical nature of the source.

### 52) [2611.00190](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00190v2): Developing Environments: Formation and Quenching at Cosmic Noon Traced by the Large-scale Distribution of Gas and Galaxies

<p> Amir H. Khoram, Sirio Belli, Trevor Mendel, Letizia Bugiani, Matteo Sapori, Andrew B. Newman, Hossein Hatamnia, Finn Giddings, Rebecca L. Davies, Olga Cucciati, Bianca Poggianti, Amirnezam Amiri, Minjung Park, Anita Zanella, Tianmu Gao, Gabriel Maheson, Stefano Sotira, Caterina Liboni, Giulia Papini </p>
<p>
Environmental density correlates with galaxy properties in the local Universe, but how and when these trends emerge is unclear: at cosmic noon, large-scale structures are still assembling and galaxies quench rapidly for debated reasons. We study how the formation and quenching histories of massive galaxies at 1.7&lt;z&lt;3.5 depend on environment, using two independent tracers: galaxy number density (δ_\#) from COSMOS-Web and intergalactic neutral hydrogen (δ_HI, cool gas) from LATIS Lyα tomography. We combine 139 Blue Jay JWST/NIRSpec galaxies with 57 DAWN JWST Archive prism spectra in COSMOS, deriving stellar masses, star formation histories, dust attenuation, and Na \sc d outflow rates from joint spectro-photometric ttProspector fits. In massive quiescent galaxies, the formation-to-quenching interval increases with density. AGN and Na \sc d outflows are confined to δ_\#&lt;0.5 and elevated Lyα absorption (δ_HI&gt;1), mostly in massive quiescent galaxies showing both. About 65&percnt; of the sample follows a correlation between the tracers; two outlier regions stand out. A galaxy-rich, gas-poor region (δ_\#&gt;2, δ_HI&lt;1) coincides with one of the most massive Hyperion protocluster cores, where the gas is plausibly fully ionized; its quiescent galaxies formed earlier and lack significant AGN activity and outflows. A galaxy-poor, gas-rich region (δ_\#&lt;1, δ_HI&gt;1), within 60 cMpc of the densest Hyperion cores, hosts rapidly quenched galaxies with a high incidence of AGN and outflows. This phdeveloping environment, distinct from both mature protoclusters and the field, likely traces massive halos still assembling. Galaxy counts alone cannot capture the environment at cosmic noon: jointly mapping galaxies and cool gas is crucial to understand how environment regulates gas accretion, star formation, and quenching.
</p>

### 53) [2610.41584](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41584v1): Co-evolution of AGN and Host Galaxies across Cosmic Time Using JWST data

<p> Priyanka Jalan, Tomotsugu Goto, Tetsuya Hashimoto, Angel Rodriguez Barbosa, Chih-Teng Ling, Ece Kilerci, Terry Long Phan, Srivardini Ayyappan </p>
<p>
Understanding the complex co-evolution of active galactic nuclei (AGN) and their host galaxies across cosmic time remains a fundamental challenge, particularly in identifying how the physical drivers of galaxy diversity evolve from the early Universe to the present day. To understand this, we use a multi-wavelength JWST catalog of 2,709 sources cross-matched from the SMILES and JWST Advanced Deep Extragalactic Survey (JADES) surveys, spanning lookback times from 0.8 to 12.9 Gyr (0.05 &lt;z &lt;6). Using Pearson correlation matrix, we find AGN luminosity (L_AGN) strongly correlates (~0.8) with star formation rate (SFR) and dust luminosity (L_dust), weakly correlates (~0.5) with stellar mass (M<sub>&sext;</sub>) and anti-correlates (~ -0.4) with star formation history (SFH) duration and stellar age (Age<sub>&sext;</sub>). However, the apparent anti-correlation observed between galaxy age and AGN luminosity is driven by cosmic time rather than by a direct physical connection. Using Principal Component Analysis (PCA), we find that galaxy diversity is fundamentally governed by two principal components: AGN and galaxy activity, and stellar age. At higher redshifts, AGN accretion, star formation, and dust emission are tightly synchronized, reflecting gas-rich conditions in the early Universe. In contrast, the present Universe exhibits a clear bimodal structure in host-dominated systems (f_AGN ≤ 0.2), separating quiescent and star-forming galaxies. Our results demonstrate that AGN luminosity is primarily governed by the host galaxy's current cold-gas supply, of which recent star formation is a close observational tracer, with galaxy mass setting the overall potential. This highlights a co-evolution scenario in which black hole growth and star formation are tightly linked, particularly at early cosmic epochs.
</p>

### 54) [2610.41557](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41557v1): The JWST/PASSAGE Survey: Metallicity Radial Gradients in Emission-Line Galaxy Pairs at Cosmic Noon with NIRISS

<p> Sijia Li, Xin Wang, Matthew A. Malkan, Tucker Jones, Vihang Mehta, Y. Sophia Dai, Matthew J. Hayes, Claudia Scarlata, Harry I. Teplitz, Benedetta Vulcani, Ayan Acharyya, Hakim Atek, Andrew J. Bunker, Farhanul Hasan, Mason S. Huberty, Zahra Sattari, Peter J. Watson </p>
<p>
Using JWST/NIRISS slitless spectroscopy from the PASSAGE program, we analyze 17 spectroscopically confirmed emission-line galaxies in candidate interacting systems at z ~ 1.7&ndash;3.2. These galaxies span stellar masses of 7.3 ≲ log(M_*/M<sub>&odot;</sub>) ≲ 10.4 and global gas-phase metallicities of 12+log(O/H) ~ 7.6&ndash;8.7. From spatially resolved emission-line maps, we measure radial metallicity gradients separately for each galaxy, finding values from approximately -0.2 to +0.45 dex kpc^-1. The mean metallicity gradients in bins of stellar mass and redshift are consistent with previous measurements, and their scatter is not significantly different from that of the heterogeneous literature compilation in either the metallicity-gradient&ndash;stellar-mass or metallicity-gradient&ndash;redshift relation. We find tentative evidence for an anti-correlation between the metallicity gradient and the metallicity offset from the mass&ndash;metallicity relation, with a best-fit slope of -0.30 ± 0.06, possibly reflecting interaction-driven variations in star formation and chemical enrichment. Galaxies in pairs with comparable component masses tend to exhibit positive metallicity gradients, whereas galaxies in unequal-mass pairs tend to exhibit negative gradients; neither trend is statistically significant in the present sample. Overall, our results reveal substantial diversity in the metallicity distributions of candidate interacting systems at cosmic noon and provide tentative evidence that galaxy interactions may influence the redistribution of gas and metals.
</p>

### 55) [2610.41491](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41491v1): OASIS III: Bursty star formation drives observed galaxy sizes at z≥4

<p> William M. Baker, Tobias J. Looser, Francesco D'Eugenio, Andrew J. Bunker, Stefano Carniani, Stéphane Charlot, Jacopo Chevallard, Mirko Curti, A. Lola Danhaive, Qiao Duan, Kevin Hainline, Jakob M. Helton, Benjamin D. Johnson, Pierluigi Rinaldi, Brant Robertson, Fengwu Sun, Yang Sun, Sandro Tacchella, James A. A. Trussler, Zihao Wu </p>
<p>
We employ JWST/NIRSpec MSA PRISM spectroscopy together with NIRCam imaging to investigate the impact of star-formation variability on galaxy sizes for a representative sample of 51 galaxies at z≥4. The OASIS survey enables us to probe low-star-formation-rate (SFR) lulling and mini-quenched galaxies down to log_10(M<sub>&sext;</sub>/M<sub>&odot;</sub>)=8.3 and log_10(SFR/M<sub>&odot;</sub> yr^-1)&lt;-1.6, i.e. more than one order of magnitude below the star-forming main sequence (MS). This enables us to probe objects that are often missed by shallower spectroscopic surveys, in addition to the MS and starburst systems, yielding an SFR- and mass-representative sample at high redshift. We derive galaxy half-light sizes using ForcePho, enabling us to resolve the highly compact nature of these sources, and we analyse their star-formation histories and interstellar medium (ISM) properties with the full-spectral-fitting code ppxf. We find a clear anti-correlation between burstiness, as traced by the ratio of the SFR from Hα compared to that from non-parametric spectral fitting averaged over 100 Myr, and galaxy UV/optical half-light radii as measured with ForcePho from a combination of well-sampled NIRCam photometry. Using partial correlation coefficients we show that the anti-correlation between burstiness (as traced by the SFR ratio) and size cannot simply be explained by secondary dependencies on stellar mass or redshift. We interpret this relation as the effect of central starbursts in SFR-rising (bursting) systems, where intense central star formation reduces measured galaxy half-light sizes. This burstiness then regulates galaxy sizes at high redshift. Should the same mechanism be present at z&gt;10, this could explain the size bimodality seen in the UV luminous galaxies as simply stemming from a more fundamental relationship between burstiness and size.
</p>

### 56) [2610.41489](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41489v1): OASIS I: Spectroscopy of Mini-Quenched and Lulling Galaxies Constrains the Quiet Side of Bursty Star Formation at z &gt; 4

<p> Tobias J. Looser, Francesco D'Eugenio, William M. Baker, Santiago Arribas, Andrew J. Bunker, Stefano Carniani, Stéphane Charlot, Jacopo Chevallard, Mirko Curti, Qiao Duan, Kevin Hainline, Jakob M. Helton, Yuki Isobe, Benjamin D. Johnson, Pierluigi Rinaldi, Brant Robertson, Fengwu Sun, Sandro Tacchella, James A. A. Trussler, Hannah Übler, Joris Witstok, Zihao Wu </p>
<p>
JWST has revealed that star formation in early galaxies is bursty, including episodes of violent suppression, so-called mini-quenching. But these were largely serendipitous discoveries in samples of UV-bright, emission-line-dominated galaxies, so that the quiet side of the burst cycle has remained poorly explored. With the JWST Cycle 3 program OASIS (GO 5997), we searched for such galaxies deliberately, using deep 28-hour NIRSpec/PRISM spectroscopy across the full burst cycle at z&gt;4. We identify three mini-quenched galaxies with log(M<sub>&sext;</sub>/M<sub>&odot;</sub>) = 8.3-8.9 and no detectable Balmer emission, at least 1.2-1.7 dex below the star-forming main sequence (SFMS), five lulling galaxies with weak Balmer emission above the quenching threshold, and 17 nearly-main-sequence galaxies just below the SFMS. Finding them required spectroscopy: seven of our ten photometric candidates for suppressed (mini-quenched and lulling) galaxies turned out to be nearly-MS or above the SFMS, and photometric fits overestimate their current star-formation rates and cannot distinguish lulling from mini-quenched ones. The suppressed galaxies are common: five of the eight come from tiers not selected on star-formation state, where they make up 29% of the galaxies at z = 4-5.5 and log(M<sub>&sext;</sub>/M<sub>&odot;</sub>) = 8-9.5, a number density of ~1.6x10^-3 Mpc^-3 dex^-1. Simulations predict them to be an order of magnitude rarer at these masses and to occur mainly at lower masses. The suppressed galaxies are also old: Balmer-break strength and stellar age both increase as galaxies fall further below the SFMS, with light-weighted ages of 5 Myr for galaxies above it, ~30 Myr for the lulling galaxies and above 100 Myr for the mini-quenched ones. They show little stellar reddening (E(B-V) &lt; 0.2), consistent with depleted gas reservoirs. Suppressed phases at z&gt;4 are therefore neither rare nor brief, but a common and lasting part of early galaxy growth.
</p>

### 57) [2610.40937](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40937v1): The Size-Mass Relation and Inside-Out Quenching of Massive Quiescent Galaxies at 2&lt;z&lt;3 with JWST PRIMER and MINERVA

<p> Vaidik Prasal, Yogesh Wadadekar, Pralay Biswas </p>
<p>
We investigate the morphology, size&ndash;mass relations, and spatially resolved star formation histories of 26 massive quiescent galaxies at cosmic noon (2 &lt; z &lt; 3) in the Ultra-Deep Survey field using deep JWST NIRCam imaging from the PRIMER and MINERVA surveys. Combining 16 broadband and medium-band filters with empirical point spread function matching, we perform single-Sérsic modeling and bulge&ndash;disk decompositions with pysersic across six filters (F115W to F444W), alongside Bayesian size&ndash;mass fitting with dynesty. In the rest-frame optical (F200W), the size&ndash;mass relation exhibits a steep slope of α= 0.59 ± 0.12 and effective radius of A = 0.83_-0.07^+0.08 kpc at M_* = 5 × 10^10 M<sub>&odot;</sub>. In the rest-frame near-infrared (F356W), we derive α= 0.56 ± 0.13 and a more compact radius of A = 0.70_-0.06^+0.07 kpc. Galaxy sizes shrink by ≈ 22&percnt; from F115W to F444W, reflecting negative color gradients and centrally concentrated mass profiles. Two-thirds of the sample are bulge-dominated (B/T ≥ 0.5), whereas one-third are disk-dominated. Central bulges are ultra-compact (R_e,Bulge ≈ 0.49 kpc) with negligible mass dependence, whereas outer disks extend to R_e,Disk ≈ 1.4&ndash;1.5 kpc. Resolved spectral energy distribution modeling with piXedfit demonstrates positive radial specific star formation rate gradients and inner regions that formed at least ≈ 0.7 Gyr earlier and quenched faster than their outskirts. These results confirm inside-out quenching, consistent with dissipative gas compaction assembling the dense core, active galactic nucleus feedback halting central star formation, and the compact bulge dynamically stabilizing the surviving disk against fragmentation.
</p>

### 58) [2610.40846](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40846v1): From Quantum Field Fluctuations to High-Redshift AGN: Semiclassical Origins of Halo Mass Function Constraints in the JWST Era

<p> Elan Yogeswaren, Amir-Homayoon Najmi, Robert K Schaefer </p>
<p>
We develop a continuous framework connecting quantum field fluctuations in the early Universe to the abundance and observable properties of galaxies and active galactic nuclei (AGN) at cosmic dawn. Beginning with quantum fields in curved Friedmann Lemaitre Robertson Walker spacetime, we follow inflationary vacuum fluctuations into the primordial curvature spectrum, linear matter power spectrum, mass variance σ(M,z), mass peak height ν(M,z), and the statistics of nonlinear halo formation. The Press-Schechter and Sheth-Tormen formalisms provide an analytic description of halo abundance and its dependence on peak rarity. A complementary high redshift halo mass function calibrated with the GUREFT simulations of Yung et al. (2024) provides a numerical description specifically constructed for the cosmic dawn regime probed by JWST. The comparison between these analytic and simulation calibrated prescriptions provides a direct measure of the sensitivity of predicted halo populations to the observations. We separate this cosmological supply of halos from the astrophysical and observational mappings that produce stars, ultraviolet luminosities, and active galactic nuclei. Controlled numerical experiments show that a percent level perturbation in the primordial fluctuation amplitude produces only a modest change in σ and ν, but can strongly amplify the exponentially suppressed high ν tail of the Halo Mass Function. This sensitivity increases toward high halo mass and high redshift, identifying rare cosmic dawn populations as potentially powerful probes of primordial statistics while simultaneously exposing the importance of uncertainties in halo abundance. High redshift AGN population statistics and the Ultraviolet Luminosity Function provide additional constraints on the primordial power spectrum and dynamics of primordial inflation theory.
</p>

### 59) [2610.40796](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40796v1): NGDEEP: Anchoring the 0.5 &lt; z &lt; 3.5 Star-Forming Main Sequence at Low Stellar Masses with Ultradeep JWST/NIRISS WFSS

<p> Claire Bolda, Casey Papovich, Lu Shen, Nikko J. Cleri, L. Y. Aaron Yung, Vicente Estrada-Carpenter, Steven L. Finkelstein, Anton M. Koekemoer, Nor Pirzkal, Stephen M. Wilkins </p>
<p>
Here we present deep JWST/NIRISS wide-field slitless spectroscopy (WFSS) that covers Hα and Hβ for galaxies at 0.5 &lt; z &lt; 3.5 in the NGDEEP survey. The NGDEEP NIRISS observations detect emission line fluxes from galaxies in the Hubble Ultra Deep Field to m_F150W ≲ 28 mag, enabling studies of star-formation based on Hα (or Hβ, if necessary) in low-mass galaxies with log(M_*/M<sub>&odot;</sub>) ≳ 7 at z = 0.5 and log(M_*/M<sub>&odot;</sub>) ≳ 8 at z = 3.5. For 284 NGDEEP galaxies with &gt;3σ detections of Hα or Hβ, we determine dust-corrected star-formation rates, SFR_Hα, via flux measurements of these Balmer lines and compare them to the galaxies' stellar masses to investigate the properties of the star-forming main sequence (SFMS) on short, ~10~Myr timescales across three redshift bins. When we contrast the intrinsic scatter in the Hα&ndash;based SFMS to that of a SFMS comprised of UV&ndash;based SFRs, which trace star formation within the past ~ 100~Myr, we find that the \Ha&ndash;based SFMS's intrinsic scatter (≈0.35-0.5~dex over 0.5 &lt; z &lt; 3.5) is ≈0.2-0.4~dex higher than the UV&ndash;based SFMS's intrinsic scatter at z &lt; 2.5. We argue that brief (≲10~Myr) bursts of star formation likely account for this disparity in SFMS scatter. Theoretical predictions from the FIRE simulations underestimate the scatter we observe in the Hα&ndash;based SFMS at 1.5 &lt; z &lt; 2.5 and log(M_*/M<sub>&odot;</sub>) &lt; 9 by up to ≈0.25~dex, which suggests that low-mass galaxies have burstier star-formation histories than expected on ≲10~Myr timescales.
</p>

### 60) [2610.40790](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40790v1): The assembly of dusty galaxies at z ≥ 4: grain growth and the evolutionary origin of the dust-to-stellar-mass scatter

<p> Alessia D'Angelo, Raffaella Schneider, Luca Graziani, Claudia Di Cesare, Rosa Valiante, Gergő Popping, Giulia Rodighiero, Laura Bisigello, Denis Burgarella, Giovanni Gandolfi, Koki Otaki, Tommaso Zana </p>
<p>
Understanding dust evolution is essential for interpreting the properties of galaxies across cosmic time. Nearby galaxies have revealed a detailed picture of dust enrichment, but whether the same picture applies in the early Universe remains unclear. Here we explore the origin of interstellar dust in simulated galaxies at z ~ 5. Starting from the dust-to-stellar mass relation, we select five simulated star-forming galaxies with SFR&gt; 1 M<sub>&odot;</sub> yr^-1 and log(M<sub>&sext;</sub>/M<sub>&odot;</sub>) &gt; 8. We focus on three low-mass systems with comparable stellar masses (log(M<sub>&sext;</sub>/M<sub>&odot;</sub>) ~ 8.7) but significantly different dust masses (log(M_dust/M<sub>&odot;</sub>) ~ 5.1-6.2), to investigate the origin of the scatter in the relation. Our analysis combines outputs from the cosmological hydrodynamical simulation dustyGadget, a semi-analytical model for stellar dust production, and radiative-transfer post-processing with SKIRT. We confirm that stellar dust production alone reproduces only the low-dust branch of the relation. Grain growth is required to recover both its S-shape and the observed scatter, as established for nearby galaxies. The transition to efficient grain growth occurs at lower metallicity than locally, consistent with the denser interstellar medium of high-redshift systems. Differences in dust content reflect different stages of galaxy assembly. Progressively longer star formation histories lead to greater chemical enrichment and denser ISM, promoting increasingly efficient grain growth. We therefore identify the assembly stage as the key regulator of dust growth at high redshift. Mock observations show that JWST multi-band imaging can reveal the structural diversity of galaxies within the scatter, although the reconstruction of clumpy morphologies may remain incomplete. Their dust continuum emission remains below the sensitivity of current ALMA continuum surveys.
</p>

### 61) [2610.40787](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40787v1): Cosmic merger rates and population evolution of binary black hole mergers in AGN disks

<p> Maria Paola Vaccaro, Alessandro Trinca, Michela Mapelli, Lumen Boco, Raffaella Schneider, Rosa Valiante, Dominika Wylezalek </p>
<p>
Active galactic nuclei (AGN) disks are promising sites for hierarchical binary black hole (BBH) mergers, which next-generation gravitational-wave detectors will probe out to high redshift. Predicting their contribution requires linking BBH evolution in AGN disks to the poorly constrained high-redshift evolution of active supermassive black holes (SMBHs). We introduce ARISE (AGN Rates and Intrinsic Stellar-BH Evolution), a framework that embeds semi-analytical models of BBH evolution in AGN disks into a cosmological description of the AGN number density, SMBH mass function, Eddington-ratio distribution, and nuclear metallicity. We consider a conservative AGN-abundance model anchored to observed luminosity functions, as well as Eddington-limited and super-Eddington scenarios based on the CAT cosmological model, compatible with JWST observations of high-redshift AGNs. The merger-rate density is set primarily by the disk viscosity parameter α: at z = 0.2, we find a rate of 12&ndash;37 Gpc^-3 yr^-1 for α= 0.01 and 0.5&ndash;6 Gpc^-3 yr^-1 for α= 0.1. The redshift evolution depends strongly on how SMBHs grow. The rate density peaks at z ≃ 1.8, 3.3, and 3.9 (1.5, 3.6, and 3.9) for α=0.01 (0.1) in the conservative, Eddington-limited, and super-Eddington models, respectively, and in the super-Eddington model it stays nearly flat out to z ≃ 10. For α= 0.1, AGN disks account for ~ 2&ndash;40&percnt; of the LVK-inferred merger rate, consistent with current upper limits from GW&ndash;AGN associations. For α= 0.01, they match or exceed it. The Einstein Telescope (ET) should detect ~ 10^5 (~ 10^4) AGN-disk mergers per year for α= 0.01 (0.1). ET will test the AGN channel at redshifts where the AGN population models differ most strongly, providing independent constraints on the growth of early SMBHs.
</p>

### 62) [2610.40770](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40770v1): When precise inference is wrong: Diagnosing model misspecification with applications to cosmic dawn and reionisation

<p> Daniela Breitman </p>
<p>
As observations of the cosmic dawn (CD) and epoch of reionisation (EoR) rapidly improve in precision, the approximate forward models used to interpret them might struggle to keep pace. Bayesian inference with a misspecified model, one that cannot represent the processes generating the observations, can yield tightly constrained, yet misleading and biased astrophysics, with different parts of the observations favouring incompatible parameter values. Model misspecification can evade a χ^2 goodness-of-fit test, and Bayesian model selection requires a better model, which is not always available. We therefore apply the suspiciousness, a statistic developed to quantify tensions between datasets, to CD/EoR inference as a model criticism tool that tests whether these parts can be explained by the same parameters. We first apply it to HERA 21-cm power spectrum (PS) upper limits, which are too weak to reveal misspecification, as expected, and to HST and JWST UV luminosity functions (LFs). Under our galaxy model, the UV LFs at z≤10 and z&gt;10 are in tension at 5.0σ in the Gaussian limit, a failure visible in the fits, although doubling the uncertainties of the z&gt;10 observations reduces it to 2.1σ. Finally, we analyse a mock SKA-Low AA<sup>&ast;</sup> 1080 h 21-cm PS with the model that generated it and with a simpler, misspecified model. We find that the misspecified model silently fails: it fits the PS well, passing visual inspection and, for most noise realisations, a χ^2 test, yet its inferred astrophysics is wrong. The Bayesian evidence disfavours it, but only relative to the true model, which is unavailable for real observations. The suspiciousness finds no tension for the well-specified model, but a ~3.4σ tension for the misspecified one, strongly ruling it out without requiring a more adequate candidate model.
</p>

### 63) [2610.40633](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40633v1): PRISMS GHZ8: Evidence for a Young Burst and Potential Merger Activity at z=10.23

<p> Ruqiu Lin, Jorge Zavala, Javier Álvarez-Márquez, Luis Colina, Marco Castellano, Adriano Fontana, Paola Santini, Lorenzo Napolitano, Sarah Kendrew, Alejandro Crespo Gómez, Victoria Salazar, Abdurro'uf, Stefano Carniani, Tiger Hsiao, Yuichi Harikane, Mahmoud Hamed, Takuya Hashimoto, Akio Inoue, Danial Langeroodi, Rui Marques-Chaves, Ikki Mitsuhashi, Yurina Nakazato, Borja Pérez-Díaz, Pierluigi Rinaldi, Carlota Prieto-Jiménez, Ana Varo-O'Ferrall, Fang-Ting Yuan, Shuairu Zhu, Zhen-Ya Zheng </p>
<p>
UV-bright galaxies in the early Universe provide important constraints on the rapid build-up of stellar mass and the sources responsible for cosmic reionization. However, the physical processes that drive their high UV luminosities remain unclear. Here we report JWST/MIRI LRS spectroscopy of GHZ8, a UV-bright galaxy at z=10.23 with a magnification μ=1.23. The spectrum shows a ~ 5σ detection for the Hβ-[OIII] complex and ~ 4σ for Hα line, with fluxes of 3.2±0.9×10^-18 and 1.7±0.4×10^-18 erg s<sup>-1</sup> cm<sup>2</sup>, respectively. Based on JWST/NIRCam imaging, we find that GHZ8 consists of two components separated only by 0.16" (≈600 pc): a bright, extended main component and a faint, compact southeastern clump, hereafter referred to as GHZ8-main and GHZ8-SE. Through morphological decomposition, we find demagnified M_UV=-19.91±0.10 and -18.03±0.15 mag for GHZ8-main and GHZ8-SE, with blue UV slopes of β_UV=-2.53±0.22 and -3.45±0.49, respectively, indicating that both components host young stellar populations. In particular, the extremely blue UV continuum of GHZ8-SE requires an extremely young, ISM-poor stellar population with a high ionizing photon escape fraction, f<sub>esc</sub>= 0.6-1.0. Combining the UV and Hα luminosities with the results from the spectro-photometric SED fitting, we constrain the star-formation activity of GHZ8-main, and find either a recent, short-lived burst of star formation that is now in its final phases, or continuous star formation over a relatively short period (&lt;50 Myr). GHZ8 therefore provides observational evidence for bursty star formation in a UV-bright galaxy. Globally, GHZ8 may be undergoing a merger-induced starburst, although deeper imaging or spatially resolved kinematic information is required for a more definitive interpretation.
</p>

### 64) [2610.37821](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37821v1): Simulation-Informed Dust Masses in Typical 6&lt;z&lt;14 JWST Galaxies

<p> Jed McKinney, Dhruv T. Zimmerman, Desika Narayanan, Caitlin M. Casey, Julian B. Munoz, Paul Torrey, Hui Li, Federico Marinacci </p>
<p>
Direct detections of dust in typical z&gt;8 galaxies remain untenable, despite the presence of dust suggested by rest-frame UV/optical spectro-photometry. Informed by cosmological zoom-in simulations where dust particles evolve on-the-fly, we present a novel method for inferring dust masses in high-redshift galaxies using stellar half-light radii and UV/optical attenuation. Our methods reproduce observed trends at lower redshift, ultimately diverging from Milky Way-based calibration factors by up to ~1.5 dex. We apply this method to observed JWST galaxies from 6&lt;z&lt;14, and find dust masses in the range M_dust~10^3-10^5 M<sub>&odot;</sub> that are fully consistent with upper limits from ALMA, and suggest an early dust enrichment period by supernovae followed by efficient grain destruction in the hot gas. Future observations may use our tabulated calibration to infer dust masses over a wide range of stellar masses and redshifts.
</p>

### 65) [2610.37751](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37751v1): A GLIMPSE of Dark Matter: JWST Constraints on Early Structure Formation

<p> Julian B. Muñoz, John Chisholm, Seiji Fujimoto, Tiger Yu-Yang Hsiao, Kristen B. W. McQuinn, Priyamvada Natarajan, A. Saldana-Lopez, Hakim Atek, Arghyadeep Basu, Danielle Berg, Ryan Endsley, Lukas J. Furtak, Damien Korber, Rohan P. Naidu, Richard Pan, Daniel Schaerer </p>
<p>
While we have a solid understanding of how dark matter (DM) behaves during recombination (z~ 10^3) and in the late universe (z≲ a few), much less is known about intermediate times, when the first galaxies formed. These galaxies provide a powerful handle on DM through structure formation, as halos have to exist to host luminous sources. Here we use recent determinations of the UV luminosity function from the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) GLIMPSE lensing program, along with legacy Hubble and Subaru data, to test the small-scale behavior of DM during the epoch of reionization (z~ 7). Marginalizing over astrophysical uncertainties, we find the matter power spectrum to be consistent with the cold-DM prediction down to sub-Mpc scales, with 1σ uncertainties of 0.2 dex at k=3-9   Mpc^-1 and 0.5 dex at k=9-27   Mpc^-1, the smallest scales probed at these early times. For thermal-relic warm DM we constrain m_WDM &gt; 3.2  keV (95% CL), independent of lower-z probes. Our results test small-scale structure formation in a new redshift window and demonstrate the promise of JWST to constrain the nature of DM.
</p>

### 66) [2610.37643](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37643v1): What shapes the bright end of the UV luminosity function? Stellar mass, star-formation variability, and dust in JADES

<p> Amanda Stoffers, Sandro Tacchella, Benjamin D. Johnson, Brant Robertson, Roberto Maiolino, Andrew J. Bunker, Emma Curtis-Lake, A. Lola Danhaive, Qiao Duan, Francesco D'Eugenio </p>
<p>
JWST surveys find more UV-bright galaxies at z&gt;10 than galaxy-formation models predict, but the roles of recent star formation, stellar mass and dust remain uncertain. We analyse 1365 spectroscopically confirmed JADES galaxies at 3≤ z≤9, fitting NIRCam photometry and NIRSpec emission lines with \ceri. Our model includes flexible star-formation histories (SFHs) under three priors, two-component dust attenuation and nebular emission. We account for spectroscopic selection through population reweighting and assess the reliability of inferred trends. At z=3&ndash;5, rising SFHs are more common in low-mass galaxies, whereas most galaxies above 10^9.5 M<sub>&odot;</sub> have declining SFHs. The apparent increase in rising fractions with redshift cannot be distinguished from the adopted SFH prior alone. At fixed stellar mass, recent star formation is the strongest predictor of UV brightness. UV-brighter galaxies have higher recent SFRs, younger stellar populations and less attenuation at fixed SFR. Below 10^9 M<sub>&odot;</sub>, they are also more likely to have rising SFHs, with odds increasing by up to a factor of 2.3 per magnitude brighter, although the strongest trend requires caution. In contrast, galaxies with M_UV&lt;-20 at z=3&ndash;5 are predominantly massive, and approximately half have declining SFHs. Dust partly offsets burst-driven UV brightening: attenuation increases with recent SFR and declines gradually after the inferred SFR peak. In model sensitivity tests, dust has the largest effect on bright-end counts, whereas removing galaxy-to-galaxy SFH scatter changes them by at most 0.17~dex. Thus, bursts can make low-mass galaxies unusually UV-bright for their mass, while the absolute bright end of the UVLF is primarily shaped by stellar mass and dust attenuation.
</p>

### 67) [2610.37612](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37612v2): The Role of Star Formation and Morphology in the Mass-Metallicity Relation of Galaxies at Cosmic Noon

<p> Farhanul Hasan, Marc Rafelski, Kalina V. Nedkova, Peter J. Watson, Debra M. Elmegreen, Bruce G. Elmegreen, Zahra Sattari, Vihang Mehta, Alaina Henry, Mitchell Revalski, Ayan Acharyya, Benedetta Vulcani, Grecco A. Oyarzun, Y. Sophia Dai, Xin Wang, Matthew A. Malkan, Claudia M. Scarlata, Matthew J. Hayes, Alexandra Le Reste, Andrew J. Battisti, Anahita Alavi, James Colbert, Hakim Atek, Michael Rutkowski, Tucker Jones, Ilias Goovaerts, Alexander Beckett, Andrew J. Bunker, Mason Huberty </p>
<p>
We investigate the dependence of the mass-metallicity relation (MZR) on star formation and galaxy morphology at 1.7&lt;z&lt;3.5, using JWST/NIRISS wide-field slitless spectroscopy of &gt;1000 galaxies. The observations span &gt;85 arcmin<sup>2</sup> from the pure-parallel PASSAGE and OutThere surveys. We derive gas-phase metallicities using a Bayesian framework that combines multiple strong emission-line ratios calibrated against direct auroral-line metallicity measurements from JWST. We constrain stellar masses, star formation rates (SFRs), and morphological properties using ancillary HST and JWST imaging. We stack spectra by stellar mass to constrain the MZR over log(M<sub>&sext;</sub>/M)~6.5-11, and find that it is well described by a second-order polynomial, with a steep low-mass slope and metallicities&lt;10% solar at log(M<sub>&sext;</sub>/M)&lt;7.5. Additional stacking by SFR and morphology reveals strong evidence for a fundamental metallicity relation (FMR). Specifically, at log(M<sub>&sext;</sub>/M)&lt;9.5, galaxies with lower SFRs are more metal-rich than those with higher SFRs at fixed mass. At log(M<sub>&sext;</sub>/M)&lt;9.5, we find that larger galaxies, and those having lower SFR surface densities, bulges, and/or spiral features are significantly metal rich relative to their smaller, higher SFR surface density, bulge-less, and/or non-spiral counterparts. These results suggest the secondary dependencies of the MZR reflect variations in the baryon cycle, such that recent metal-poor gas accretion drives concentrated star formation and metallicity dilution, while structurally developed galaxies retain more chemically enriched gas reservoirs.
</p>

### 68) [2610.37564](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37564v1): Little Red Dots with Broad Hβ Lines Reveal the Black Hole Mass Function at 7&lt;z&lt;9.5

<p> Anthony J. Taylor, Steven L. Finkelstein, Hollis B. Akins, Vasily Kokorev, Pablo Arrabal Haro, Sophie Koudmani, Caitlin M. Casey, Mark Dickinson, Junehyoung Jeon, Madisyn Brooks, Volker Bromm, Christopher J. Conselice, Kelcey Davis, Callum T. Donnan, Andreas L. Faisst, Seiji Fujimoto, Lukas J. Furtak, Mauro Giavalisco, Ansh R. Gupta, Michaela Hirschmann, Tiger Yu-Yang Hsiao, Dale D. Kocevski, Anton M. Koekemoer, Gene C. K. Leung, Ray A. Lucas, Lorenzo Napolitano, Casey Papovich, Laura Pentericci, Pablo G. Perez-Gonzalez, Rachel S. Somerville, Fabian Walter, L. Y. Aaron Yung </p>
<p>
We present a systematic search for broad Hβ emission at 7&lt;z&lt;9.5 in 21,770 JWST/NIRSpec PRISM spectra drawn from four blank fields and six lensing clusters with photometry spanning 1942 arcmin<sup>2</sup>. We find a sample of 20 sources with statistically significant broad-Hβ emission, including 13 new sources. Our entire sample has colors and morphologies consistent with little red dots (LRDs), compared to only 37&percnt; of broad-line emitters at 3.5&lt;z&lt;6. Interpreting the broad-lines as indicators of Broad-line Active Galactic Nuclei (BLAGN), we infer black hole masses of 4.2×10^6-2.1×10^8 M<sub>&odot;</sub>. We calculate completeness corrections and construct the black hole mass function at z~8. The normalization of the black hole mass function rises by ≈1.0~dex from z~8-5, consistent with average black hole growth at f_rmEdd≈0.1. Comparing with cosmological simulations, the z~8 black hole mass function requires heavy (10^5 M<sub>&odot;</sub>) seeds or super-Eddington accretion onto light or intermediate mass seeds to match its normalization. Finally, we infer bolometric luminosities for the sample and construct a bolometric luminosity function. We integrate over this luminosity function to derive a black hole accretion density (BHAD) of 4.8^+5.8_-2.6×10^-7~M<sub>&odot;</sub>~rmyr^-1~rmMpc^-3, showing broad agreement with previous photometric work, but disagreement with previous X-ray models, reinforcing the idea that BLAGN are more common in the early universe than previously expected.

### 69) [2610.37540](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37540v1): JWST/MIRI Observations of Amorphous Silicates in Interstellar Comet 3I/ATLAS

<p> Cristina A. Thomas, Michael S. P. Kelley, David E. Harker, Martin A. Cordiner, Davide Farnocchia, Dennis Bodewits, Steven B. Charnley, Maria N. Drozdovskaya, Marco Micheli, Stefanie N. Milam, Cyrielle Opitom, Silvia Protopapa, Nathan X. Roth, Megan E. Schwamb </p>
<p>
We present JWST/MIRI Low Resolution Spectroscopy (LRS) of 3I/ATLAS obtained post-perihelion at a heliocentric distance of 2.21 au. Our spectrum contains a prominent 10 micron silicate emission feature with a broad, smooth shape that is distinct from those commonly observed in Solar System comets. We model the spectrum using thermal emission from amorphous carbon, amorphous olivine, and amorphous pyroxene grains. Our models strongly favor a pyroxene-dominated, amorphous silicate composition and show a degeneracy between silicate grain size and the relative abundance of silicate and carbon dust. Across a set of representative models, the best-fitting crystalline abundance is zero, and we place an upper limit of 5.3% on the crystalline fraction of the silicates. We identify an emission feature near 6.83 microns that is consistent with aliphatic C-H-bearing material. The overwhelmingly amorphous silicate composition supports previous work that measured unusual volatile and isotopic composition in suggesting that the material incorporated into the object experienced little thermal processing and preserves information about the conditions in which this interstellar planetesimal formed.
</p>

### 70) [2610.36651](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36651v1): Excitation and Destruction of Small Polycyclic Aromatic Hydrocarbons by the Luminous Seyfert Nucleus of NGC 4151

<p> Grant P. Donnelly, J. -D. T. Smith, Sebastian F. Hönig, Daniel A. Dale, Tanio Díaz-Santos, B. T. Draine, Brandon S. Hensley, Thomas S. -Y. Lai, Karin M. Sandstrom, Andrey Vayner </p>
<p>
We present a spatially-resolved detection of 3.3 μm emission from polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbons (PAHs) within the circumnuclear ~200 pc of the archetypal Seyfert galaxy NGC 4151, using new JWST NIRSpec/IFU spectroscopy. PAHs are detected down to the innermost 50 pc, though it is likely that PAHs also exist at smaller radii, as PAH detections closer than this seem to be limited by continuum dilution. This is contrary to expectations that PAH grains, especially the smallest grains that carry the 3.3 μm feature, could not survive near an active galactic nucleus (AGN). Furthermore, the surface brightness of PAH emission increases as radius from the nucleus decreases. We interpret this as evidence that the AGN supplies energy for PAH excitation by driving ultraviolet emission in nearby H and H<sub>2</sub> gas. We do not detect PAH emission within the narrow line region (NLR) that hosts highly ionized and outflowing gas, suggesting that the AGN may also destroy PAHs in these regions, depending on geometry. Together, these results show that an AGN has a complex, multifaceted role in the life cycle of PAHs and in PAH excitation.
</p>

### 71) [2610.36573](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36573v1): A Collimated Protostellar Outflow Likely Driven by Jet and Reshaped by Environment in the Sagittarius C Cloud of the Central Molecular Zone

<p> Sen-Di Bo, Xing Lu, Tian-Yu Tu, Weiyuan Zhang, Samuel Crowe, Junhao Liu, Shanghuo Li, John Bally </p>
<p>
The Central Molecular Zone (CMZ) at the center of the Galaxy provides a nearby laboratory for studying star formation under extreme physical and chemical conditions. Recent ALMA (Atacama Large Millimeter/Submillimeter Array) observations have identified 43 candidate protostellar outflows in three CMZ clouds. One of these, the Sgr C-F#2 outflow in Sagittarius C (Sgr C), has a highly collimated lobe extending approximately 0.8 pc in projection. Here we present a joint analysis of ALMA observations of SiO J=8-7, 5-4, and 2-1. We use local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE) and non-LTE methods to constrain the SiO column densities, H2 number density, and gas temperature. The low SiO rotational temperatures and higher kinetic temperatures inferred from non-LTE methods suggest subthermal excitation. The elongated SiO morphology, shock-excited gas, position-velocity structure, and the alignment of the red shifted molecular lobe with JWST infrared knots altogether favor jet-dominated entrainment, whereas a broader disk-wind component close to the source cannot be excluded. The outflow shows asymmetries in its morphology and physical properties, which can be attributed to dense gas on the path of its lobe impeding its propagation and feedback from a nearby HII region imposing different pressure on its two sides.
</p>

### 72) [2610.36089](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36089v1): Heavy Seeds and the Multi-scale Fueling of Early Supermassive Black Holes and Little Red Dots

<p> Junehyoung Jeon, Volker Bromm, Boyuan Liu, Stella S. R. Offner, Anthony J. Taylor, Steven L. Finkelstein </p>
<p>
The formation of supermassive black holes (SMBHs) in the early Universe has been a longstanding mystery due to the brief period available to assemble them. More recently, the unexpected discovery with the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) of Little Red Dots (LRDs), compact sources exhibiting signatures of active galactic nuclei (AGN), has accentuated this challenge. Cosmological simulations investigating their formation and evolution confront the limitation that the compact scale of SMBH/LRD physics cannot be simultaneously resolved with the larger-scale cosmological environment. We address this limitation by utilizing a cutout technique, selecting a targeted region around an SMBH seed from a cosmological parent simulation and re-running the cutout at higher (sub-pc) resolution for shorter periods. We explore two SMBH formation scenarios: the standard, light seed stellar-remnant case, and the heavy-seed, direct collapse black hole (DCBH) channel. Within the high-resolution achieved by the cutouts, the DCBH, due to its larger mass, is able to induce a buildup of high density gas nearby and accrete gas in bursts of super-Eddington phases, growing in mass and producing an LRD-like spectrum. In contrast, the stellar remnant cannot do so and remains dormant, unable to affect the nearby gas or significantly evolve by gas accretion alone. Therefore, we find that observed high-redshift SMBHs and LRDs require the existence of heavy seeds to assemble dense gas around themselves, enabling efficient growth and producing red optical spectra with strong Balmer breaks resembling LRDs.
</p>

### 73) [2610.36046](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36046v1): Does the Escape Fraction Prescription Shape Reionization History and Morphology?

<p> Pulkit Sheoran, Ankita Bera, Sultan Hassan </p>
<p>
The escape fraction of ionizing photons, f<sub>esc</sub>, is one of the least constrained quantities of the Epoch of Reionization (EoR), and the opacity of the intergalactic medium prevents its direct measurement at high redshifts. We constrain three power-law parameterizations, f<sub>esc</sub>(z), f<sub>esc</sub>(M_h), and f<sub>esc</sub>(z, M_h), against JWST UV luminosity functions and EoR observables (the ionizing emissivity, the neutral hydrogen fraction, and the Thomson optical depth) using Markov Chain Monte Carlo, with priors that allow f<sub>esc</sub> to either increase or decrease with redshift and halo mass. We then propagate the best-fit models into the semi-numerical simulation SimFast21 and compare their ionization morphology and 21 cm power spectra with those of a constant-f<sub>esc</sub> model. All models reproduce the observations with nearly identical global reionization histories. The data favor an escape fraction that decreases with increasing redshift, from ≈ 0.8 at z = 5 to ≈ 0.35 at z = 15. The halo-mass-only model prefers f<sub>esc</sub> increasing with halo mass, but this trend acts as a proxy for redshift evolution. When we vary both dependencies jointly, the data prefer a mass-independent f<sub>esc</sub>. At matched global neutral fraction, the ionization morphology is closely similar for all four prescriptions, and the 21 cm power spectra agree to within ≈ 10&percnt;. These results suggest that, once calibrated against the same observations, the details of the f<sub>esc</sub> prescription, which are set by the physics of the interstellar medium, largely average out in both the global observables and the 21 cm power spectrum. Distinguishing them may require statistics beyond the power spectrum, such as sightline statistics of the Lyα and 21 cm forests, or forecasts that exploit their small differences in reionization timing.
</p>

### 74) [2610.36040](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36040v1): The Stellar Populations of Resolved Galaxies

<p> Daniel R. Weisz, Alessandro Savino, Evan D. Skillman </p>
<p>
The study of galaxies outside the Milky Way using observations of resolved stellar populations has experienced extraordinary growth over the past two decades. There now exist panchromatic photometry and precise astrometry for hundreds of millions of individually resolved stars in hundreds of galaxies that span ~10^9 in luminosity, ~10^3 in metallicity, and sample a wide range of environments. Here, we review advances in extragalactic astronomy enabled by resolved stellar population imaging. We summarize major observational programs and focus on four interconnected science areas: the star formation histories of galaxies, 'bursty' star formation, the stellar initial mass function, and the orbital histories of Local Group galaxies. We review the literature in each area, synthesize key findings, identify emerging trends, and discuss how new and future facilities (e.g., JWST, LSST, Euclid, Roman, CSST, ELTs, HWO) will enable further progress. Finally, we highlight shortcomings in current datasets and discuss promising directions for new areas of investigation.
</p>

We find that adding medium bands from the SPAM and MINERVA programs improves photometric redshifts. We discuss how these data advance the SPAM survey's key science goals: better-constrained photometric redshifts, identification of high-redshift overdensity members, improved recovery of emission-line strengths, and more accurate star formation histories.

### 76) [2610.34552](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34552v1): Multiphase Gas Structure in JWST Little Red Dots

<p> Seok-Jun Chang, Max Gronke, Jorryt Matthee, Charlotte Mason </p>
<p>
JWST has uncovered a population of compact high-redshift galaxies, 'little red dots' (LRDs), exhibiting broad Balmer emission lines that often show narrow absorption features and strong Balmer breaks - spectral signatures attributed to dense gas surrounding rapidly growing black holes, but whose small-scale structure remains unknown. A striking feature of LRD spectra is that the Hβ absorption is systematically deeper than Hα, yet in any homogeneous gas this ordering is physically impossible. Here we model the Hα and Hβ line profiles using radiative transfer simulations for 11 LRDs with JWST sensitive high resolution spectroscopy, and independently constrain the n=2 hydrogen column density, N_HI,\mathitn=2, from the Balmer-break continuum. We find that the N_HI,\mathitn=2 from Hα in a homogeneous medium cannot reproduce the observed Hβ absorption in several objects, and that this N_HI,\mathitn=2 typically falls two orders of magnitude below the lower bound set by the Balmer break. Both tensions are simultaneously resolved by a multiphase medium of dense clumps within diffuse hot ionized gas. This multiphase gas structure is a necessary ingredient for any viable LRD model, regardless of the nature of the central engine.
</p>

### 77) [2610.34441](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34441v1): Theoretical Pulsation Properties of RR Lyrae and Type II Cepheids in the JWST and Roman photometric systems

<p> M. Marconi, R. Molinaro, A. Bhardwaj, I. Musella, V. Ripepi, E. Trentin, G. De Somma, T. Sicignano, M. Deka, M. Gholami, E. Luongo, M. Dall Ora, S. Leccia </p>
<p>
RR Lyrae (RRL) stars and Type II Cepheids (T2Cs) are key standard candles and tracers of old stellar populations. The deep, wide-field capabilities of facilities such as the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) and the Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope will significantly expand their utility as a Population II alternative to Classical Cepheids for distance scale calibrations. We present theoretical pulsation properties-including light-curve morphologies, amplitudes, and multi-filter relations for RRLs (Z = 0.0001 - 0.02, M = 0.51 - 0.80, M<sub>&odot;</sub>) and T2Cs (Z = 0.0001 - 0.01, M = 0.495 - 0.65, M<sub>&odot;</sub>) to enable detailed studies of Galactic and extragalactic populations. Using alreday available non-linear convective pulsation models, we converted their predicted bolometric light curves across various chemical compositions and evolutionary stages into the JWST and Roman photometric systems. From these, we derived multi-filter Period-Amplitude and Color-Color diagrams, alongside Period-Luminosity and Period-Wesenheit relations with explicit metallicity terms. RRL relations display a significantly stronger zero-point metallicity dependence than those of T2Cs, despite sharing similar slopes. Consequently, T2Cs offer a distance scale indicator that is remarkably robust against metallicity uncertainties. As a preliminary test, we applied our metal-dependent Period-Wesenheit relations to a subset of JWST-observed RRLs in the WLM galaxy, deriving a distance modulus of mu_0 = 24.85 +/- 0.03 mag (assuming the literature average metallicity). The derived distance to WLM is consistent with most literature values, though slightly shorter than determinations based on Classical Cepheids and the Tip of the Red Giant Branch. These theoretical relations pave the way for future high-precision distance determinations and asteroseismic diagnostics with JWST and Roman.
</p>

<p> Letizia Bugiani, Andrew B. Newman, Sirio Belli, Cosimo Marconcini, Pietro Bergamini, Sai Gangula, Claudio Grillo, Andrea Bolamperti, Katherine E. Whitaker, Aliza Beverage, Richard S. Ellis, Amir H. Khoram, Allison Matthews, Marco Lombardi </p>
<p>
We analyze JWST/NIRSpec IFU spectroscopy of MRG-M0150, a gravitationally lensed, massive quiescent galaxy at z=2.6 from the INQUEST-JWST survey. Thanks to the combination of lensing magnification with JWST's angular resolution, we are able to build spatially resolved maps of the ionized gas spatial distribution, kinematics, and ionization properties in this system. We detect ionized gas emission throughout the galaxy: the lack of residual star formation inferred from line-ratio diagnostic diagrams, together with the decoupling between the gas and stellar kinematics, suggest that the ionized gas is in a wind. We use the 3D gas kinematics modeling code ttMOKA3D to constrain the geometry and physical properties of the ionized gas wind. We find that the observations are consistent with a biconical wind driven by the low-luminosity AGN at the center of the galaxy, which is detected in X-rays. The wind reaches high velocities of 1000-1400 km/s, implying that part of the gas may escape the halo. The ionized gas mass outflow rate, (0.6 ± 0.2)~M<sub>&odot;</sub>~yr^-1, is modest but exceeds the upper limit on the residual star formation rate, and it is likely that the wind is multiphase and carries more mass. We show that typical unresolved observations would not detect the wind, suggesting that the incidence of such outflows may be higher than previously recognized. Simple geometric arguments show that the observed outflow must have been launched ≈ 0.45~Myr ago from the time of observation. This evidence suggests that low-level AGN activity is an important mechanism for maintaining quiescence in massive galaxies at cosmic noon, potentially over cosmological timescales.
</p>

### 79) [2610.33166](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33166v1): How Steep is your Dust? Direct Measurements of Nebular Attenuation Curves at Cosmic Noon with JWST

<p> Gabriel Maheson, Sirio Belli, Sandro Tacchella, Letizia Bugiani, Joel Leja, Rebecca Davies, Minjung Park, Amir H. Khoram, Matteo Sapori, Caterina Liboni, Benjamin P. Collins </p>
<p>
Tracing the shape of the nebular attenuation curve, which is crucial for accurately measuring intrinsic emission-line fluxes, has proven challenging both in the local universe and at high redshift. Here we present nebular dust attenuation curves measured directly for 24 star-forming galaxies (9.3&lt;log(M&sext;/M<sub>&odot;</sub>)&lt;11.1) at cosmic noon (1.8&lt;z&lt;3.3) from the Blue Jay survey. Using deep JWST/NIRSpec spectra, we measure up to eight hydrogen recombination lines from Hδ to Paβ per galaxy and find little diversity in the curve shape, with most galaxies agreeing with the \citetcalzetti_dust_2000 attenuation curve. For over 90&percnt; of the sample, the nebular reddening from the Balmer lines is consistent with that from the Paschen lines. Using ttProspector spectral energy distribution (SED) fits, we obtain the stellar reddening and find that the difference between stellar and nebular reddening increases with stellar mass, and that each reddening measurement is independent of inclination, which disagrees with the two-component dust model. We then measure the impact of using the derived nebular attenuation curve or a literature curve when determining metallicity using the strong-line method, and find that for some diagnostics (e.g., S32, S3, S23, and S3O3), the difference can be as large as 5σ. Caution is needed when using these diagnostics if the nebular attenuation curve cannot be directly measured, since the resulting uncertainty can exceed the measurement uncertainty on the emission-line fluxes even with high signal-to-noise Balmer decrement measurements. These results show that the shape of the nebular attenuation curve is poorly understood and that constraining it is important for reducing systematic uncertainty in galaxy measurements.
</p>
