### 119) [2610.20722](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20722v1): An Attenuation Bump at z=9.3: Probing Dust Diversity at Cosmic Dawn

<p> Giulia Rodighiero, Santiago Álvarez-Córdoba, Michele Catone, Borja Pérez-Díaz, Andrea Ferrara, Giorgia Girardi, Lorenzo Napolitano, Paola Santini, Hollis B. Akins, Caitlin M. Casey, Maximilien Franco, Pietro Benotto, Rachana Bhatawdekar, Alessandro Bianchetti, Laura Bisigello, Daniela Calzetti, Paolo Cassata, Marco Castellano, Christopher J. Conselice, Poulomi Dam, Pratika Dayal, Andrea Enia, Gaia Edes Esposito, Andreas L. Faisst, Giovanni Gandolfi, Steven Gillman, Andrea Grazian, Carlotta Gruppioni, Michaela Hirschmann, Edoardo Iani, Kohei Inayoshi, Jeyhan S. Kartaltepe, Georgios Magdis, Alessandro Marconi, Crystal L. Martin, Bo Milvang-Jensen, Annagrazia Puglisi, Alvio Renzini, R. Michael Rich, Pierluigi Rinaldi, Brant E. Robertson, Stephen Serjeant, Irene Shivaei, Sune Toft, Amelia Vietri, Benedetta Vulcani, Fabian Walter, Stephen M. Wilkins, Jorge A. Zavala </p>
<p>
We report the identification of a UV attenuation bump in the Black Pearl, a galaxy at a spectroscopic redshift of 9.3335 observed with JWST/NIRSpec as part of the ZENITH and EMBER surveys. This is the highest-redshift UV bump reported to date, probing dust properties only approximately 500 Myr after the Big Bang. The feature is directly detected as a broad depression in the rest-frame UV continuum and is well described by a local Drude profile, with a central wavelength of 2132+/-17 Angstroms, an FWHM of 413+/-54 Angstroms, and a peak attenuation of 0.21+/-0.02 mag. Joint spectrophotometric modeling with a flexible attenuation law independently recovers a pronounced UV bump without imposing its presence a priori, regardless of the adopted star-formation history. The modeling indicates a relatively low-mass, actively star-forming galaxy, with a logarithmic stellar mass of 8.35 (+0.09/-0.08) in solar units, a star-formation rate of 2.36 (+0.54/-0.42) solar masses per year, and a visual attenuation of 0.34 (+0.12/-0.11) mag. Rest-frame optical emission-line diagnostics indicate a hard ionizing spectrum and allow for a moderate AGN contribution, but provide no evidence for a powerful AGN capable of dominating the observed UV continuum. The bump is blueshifted by 43+/-17 Angstroms (approximately 2.5 sigma) relative to the canonical Milky Way 2175 Angstrom feature and by 127 +/- 17 Angstroms relative to the few UV bumps currently known at redshift approximately 7, while its width is consistent with the broad distribution measured along Milky Way sightlines. The displaced central wavelength therefore provides the clearest evidence for a nonstandard UV-bump profile at redshift 9.3335, pointing to substantial diversity in the properties of UV-bump carriers at early cosmic times, with both PAHs and small graphite grains remaining plausible candidates.
</p>

### 120) [2610.20709](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20709v1): How Little Red Dots Turn Blue: Evolution of Kelvin-Helmholtz Contracting Black Hole Envelopes and Observational Diagnostics

<p> Zihao Wang, Kohei Inayoshi </p>
<p>
James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) observations have revealed compact, red-colored, broad-line emitters known as little red dots (LRDs), while a bluer counterpart population has been referred to as little blue dots (LBDs). Their physical connection remains unclear: they may represent different evolutionary stages of the same phenomenon, distinct populations, or different phases of the broader evolution of early galaxies and their black holes (BHs). We propose a model in which a rapidly growing BH is embedded in a geometrically thick gaseous envelope accompanied by concurrent nuclear star formation. The model reproduces the observed spectral energy distributions (SEDs) of LRDs and LBDs, and naturally connects the two phases through Kelvin-Helmholtz contraction of the envelope and exposure of the nuclear accretion disk, as the gas supply declines due to external stellar activity or feedback. We predict the corresponding evolutionary tracks in the UV-optical slope (β_UV-β_opt) plane which bridge the apparent gap between LRDs and LBDs. These evolutionary tracks start from the LRD regime and consistently exhibit a red excursion that extends toward (β_UV,β_opt)~(2,-1) before turning toward the LBD regime. This feature is largely insensitive to variations in the model parameters that govern the envelope structure, providing a robust signature of the transitional population. The transition lasts ~5-50 Myr, depending primarily on the BH feedback efficiency and the gas-retention efficiency of the envelope. The abundance ratio of transitional systems to LRDs therefore provides a constraint on BH feedback and gas retention during the rapid growth of early supermassive BHs.
</p>

### 121) [2610.20697](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20697v1): A Vision for Little Red Dots in the Low-Redshift Universe with ALMA2040

<p> Fengwu Sun, Xiaojing Lin, Miguel Pereira-Santaella, Dieu D. Nguyen </p>
<p>
The discovery of Little Red Dots (LRDs) at high redshifts with JWST has revealed a previously unknown phase of black-hole growth. However, their black-hole (BH) masses, BH-to-stellar mass ratios, cold-gas reservoirs, and depletion times remain uncertain. The recent discovery of low-redshift LRDs at z≃0.1 - 0.9 creates a decisive opportunity to access their low-J CO lines for measuring molecular gas reservoirs, and the nearest systems can in principle be spatially resolved. We use J1025+1402 ("The Egg") at z=0.1007, the best-studied local LRD, as a benchmark to explore the scientific opportunities with ALMA2040. Assuming a molecular gas mass of 10^9 M<sub>&odot;</sub>, the CO(2-1) detectability strongly depends on the assumed CO-to-H<sub>2</sub> conversion (α_CO). With a large α_CO ≃ 315 - 725 M<sub>&odot;</sub> (K km s<sup>-1</sup> pc^2)^-1 at a low gas-phase metallicity, the CO line would remain very challenging for the current ALMA to detect, while ALMA2040 will likely deliver a robust CO detection and thus a gas-reservoir constraint for The Egg. Furthermore, with the best angular resolution at its CO(2-1) frequency (14.6 mas, or 27 pc), ALMA2040 may spatially resolve the CO emission from The Egg. Assuming an optimistic, SMC-like α_CO with a 50-hour deep integration, we produce mock position-velocity diagrams that may distinguish a 10^7 M<sub>&odot;</sub> black hole from the no-BH model through its steeper central velocity structure. We conclude that ALMA2040 has the potential to directly measure the gas masses, depletion times, and excitation of local LRDs, while the CO-bright subset may ultimately yield dynamical constraints on the mass of their central black holes.
</p>

### 122) [2610.20692](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20692v1): JWST Observations of RR Lyrae in Leo P

<p> Catherine M. Slaughter, Alessandro Savino, Meredith Durbin, Andrew E. Dolphin, Kristen B. W. McQuinn, Max J. B. Newman, Evan D. Skillman, Daniel R. Weisz </p>
<p>
We analyze co-spatial HST/ACS and JWST/NIRCam time-series photometric observations of RR Lyrae in the low-metallicity dwarf galaxy Leo P. This study provides a new opportunity to explore the properties of and potential for metal-poor RR Lyrae studies with JWST using well-understood relationships from past HST work. We identify 20 RR Lyrae in the HST imaging and 15 RR Lyrae in the JWST imaging. We determine the periods, amplitudes, and mean magnitudes for each of the RR Lyrae. We fit the Period-Wesenheit-Metallicity (PWZ) function to the HST RR Lyrae population, and obtain a Gaia-consistent RR Lyrae-based distance to Leo P of μ= 26.04±0.07 (1.61±0.06 Mpc), consistent with existing distance measurements. We determine a JWST-based PWZ using Leo P data along with previous JWST RR Lyrae observations in WLM. The simultaneous fit decreases the mean fractional uncertainty by 31% on the slope and by 11% on the intercept, increasing the constraining power as compared to the existing calibration.
</p>

### 123) [2610.20332](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20332v1): IRAC-Excess Galaxies in the NIRSpec Wide Survey: physical properties of bright EELGs at z~7 and implications for Reionization

<p> Katherine Ormerod, Renske Smit, Joris Witstok, Anna de Graaff, Michael V. Maseda, Andrew J. Bunker, Chris Willott </p>
<p>
We present an analysis of 23 Spitzer/IRAC excess galaxies at 5.66 &lt; z &lt; 7.64, observed as part of the NIRSpec Wide GTO survey. With NIRSpec PRISM spectroscopy, we measure a median rest-frame [OIII]+Hβ EW =1583~Å, consistent with predictions from the pre-JWST selection and we identify 17/23 (~74&percnt;) of our sample as extreme emission line galaxies (EELGs), based on a selection of [OIII]+Hβ EW ≥1000~Å. The typical EELG is powered by star-formation rather than active galactic nuclei (AGN) based on their line widths, line ratios and absence of high-ionization lines, though we cannot exclude an AGN contribution in a few individual sources. We find that our luminous (&langle; M_UV&rangle;=-20.7) EELG sample is characterised by sub-solar metallicities (7.38 &lt; 12 + log(O/H) &lt; 8.12), young stellar populations (t<sub>&sext;</sub> ~ 20 Myr), and enhanced ionizing photon production efficiencies, with median log_10(ξ<sub>ion</sub>/erg^-1~Hz) = 25.62. We find that more UV luminous (~ L_UV, z=7<sup>&ast;</sup>) EELGs exhibit systematically lower O32 ratios than fainter (~ 0.1× L_UV<sup>&ast;</sup>) EELGs at fixed [OIII]+Hβ EW, indicating less extreme radiation fields and/or lower gas densities than their fainter counterparts. Furthermore, bright EELGs exhibit low neutral hydrogen column densities, consistent with a scenario in which they have largely ionized their circumgalactic medium (CGM). Despite this, we find very low Lyman-α escape fractions (&lt;2&percnt;), suggesting these systems reside in relatively small (≲ 0.25~pMpc) ionized regions of the intergalactic medium (IGM), possibly suggesting that bright galaxies have a minor contribution to reionization.
</p>

### 124) [2610.19937](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19937v1): The Role of Environment in Shaping the Assembly Timelines of Massive Quiescent Galaxies at Cosmic Noon

<p> Tianmu Gao, J. Trevor Mendel, Letizia Bugiani, Minjung Park, Sirio Belli, Joel Leja, Claudia del P. Lagos, Amir H. Khoram, Rebecca L. Davies, Razieh Emami, Gabriel Maheson, Anita Zanella, Benjamin P. Collins </p>
<p>
JWST observations of massive quiescent galaxies (MQGs) at z~2 have revealed diverse star formation histories (SFHs). In this letter, we investigate whether these variations are linked to the environment in which MQGs reside, as quantified by the host dark matter halo mass. We identify 12 MQGs from a representative sample of galaxies at 1.6&lt;z&lt;3.5 obtained by the Blue Jay survey, a JWST Cycle 1 program, and infer their SFHs via modelling of the deep NIRSpec medium-resolution spectra along with multi-band photometry. By correlating the formation and quenching epochs of individual MQGs with their respective host halo masses from the 3D-HST survey, we find that after accounting for the redshift range of the sample (1.8&lt;z&lt;2.5), MQGs in more massive haloes formed at significantly earlier cosmic epochs. Meanwhile, although these galaxies also quenched earlier overall, the scatter in quenching epochs across similar halo masses indicates that the quenching process likely involves other contributing factors and is not solely determined by halo mass.
</p>

### 125) [2610.19926](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19926v1): Sleeping or Dead? Probing the Quenching Mechanism of JWST Dormant Galaxies at z &gt; 3 with ALMA

<p> Mengyuan Xiao, Songnan Qi, Pascal A. Oesch, Longji Bing, Miroslava Dessauges-Zavadsky, Andreas L. Faisst, Mahsa Sanati, Rashmi Gottumukkala, Gabriel Brammer, David Elbaz, Robert Feldmann, Yoshinobu Fudamoto, Lucas C. Kimmig, Benjamin Magnelli, Rui Marques-Chaves, Anurag Amol Sawarkar, Sune Toft, Francesco Valentino, Christina C. Williams </p>
<p>
JWST has revealed a population of "dormant'' galaxies at z&gt;3 with strong Balmer breaks and weak emission lines, whose star formation appears to have declined only recently. Whether these galaxies are undergoing a lasting transition toward quiescence or merely experiencing a temporary suppression of star formation remains unclear. We investigate their molecular gas content using archival ALMA observations of [CII]158um and CO rotational transitions. Starting from all publicly available JWST/NIRSpec prism observations and using joint spectrophotometric fitting, we construct a parent sample at z&gt;3 and log(Mstar/M<sub>&odot;</sub>)&gt;9.5 in the ALMA-accessible COSMOS, UDS, and GOODS-South fields. Our final sample comprises 7 dormant galaxies and 15 quiescent galaxies with ALMA constraints on their molecular gas content (mostly 3 sigma upper limits). We find that dormant galaxies exhibit systematically lower star formation rates averaged over the past 10Myr (SFR10Myr) than over the past 100Myr (SFR100Myr), supporting a recent rapid decline in their star formation. For the ~70% of the sample with sufficiently constraining ALMA observations, their molecular gas fractions lie at least ~0.3-2dex below those expected for main-sequence galaxies. Moreover, the molecular gas fractions of dormant galaxies show no systematic difference from those of quiescent galaxies in our sample. This indicates that dormant galaxies have already reached a gas-poor state shortly after quenching and favors a scenario in which the rapid shutdown of star formation is associated with depletion or removal of the molecular gas reservoir, rather than primarily with suppressed star formation efficiency while retaining a substantial molecular gas reservoir. Our results suggest that, if no substantial gas replenishment occurs in the future, dormant galaxies are more consistent with becoming "dead'' than with being temporarily "sleeping''.
</p>

### 126) [2610.19881](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19881v1): PDRs4All XXIII. JWST-MIRI template spectra of PAHs in the Orion Bar obtained with blind signal separation

<p> P. Amiot, O. Berné, J. Roux-Morin, I. Schroetter, C. Boersma, A. Fuente, J. R. Goicoechea, E. Habart, A. Maragkoudakis, T. Onaka, E. Peeters, M. Robberto, A. G. G. M Tielens, D. Van De Putte, S. Vicente </p>
<p>
The aromatic infrared bands (AIBs) are observed in a wide variety of astrophysical sources, and now in unprecedented detail by the James Webb space telescope (JWST). These bands are generally attributed to the emission from polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbon (PAHs). Our aim is to extract mid-infrared AIB template spectra from JWST observations of the Orion Bar photo-dissociation region that can be used to derive PAH properties from observations of other objects. We used the JWST MIRI-MRS observations from the PDRs4All program. We first isolated the AIBs from the data using a multi-component fitting approach. We then applied a non-negative matrix factorization to the isolated AIB spectra and extracted two templates, named AIBirrad and AIBshield following previous work. The spatial distribution and spectral characteristics of these two spectra suggest that they originate from two chemical distinct populations. We interpret AIBirrad as emission originating from a PAH population strongly exposed to ultra-violet (UV) radiation and mainly composed of large, compact ionized PAHs. Conversely, AIBshield is interpreted as originating from a more shielded population of PAHs, which is less exposed to the UV by being more embedded in the cloud. This population consists of small, asymmetric and mainly neutral PAHs with bonded aliphatic side groups. These templates can serve to analyze PAH emission in a wide variety of objects observed with MIRI-MRS.
</p>

### 127) [2610.19838](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19838v1): A heterogeneous set of unusual quasars selected from astrometry and broad-band photometry

<p> C. Cheng, K. Valeckas, L. Christensen, A. Henderson de la Fuente, L. Fuglsang, V. Vuolteennaho, Pyykkinen, R. H. Rasmussen, S. Bijavara Seshashayana, J. P. U. Fynbo </p>
<p>
The aim of this study is to explore methods for how to identify new quasars with different properties with regard to reddening, redshift, the presence of broad absorption lines from outflows, or other rare properties affecting the broad-band colours. We use Gaia-based astrometric pre-selection to study only sources that have zero parallax and zero proper motion within errors. We subsequently match these sources with information from wide-area photometric surveys and build colour-colour diagrams. Based on these diagrams we select targets for follow-up spectroscopy. In the subsequent analysis we also apply observations from SPHEREx to firmly establish redshifts. We secure spectroscopic observations for 24 sources. Of these, three are found to be stars and the remaining 21 are classified as quasars spanning a redshift range from z=0.139 to z=3.732. The majority of the sources located away from the normal quasar locus in colour-colour diagrams are found to be broad-absorption-line quasars. A single source, the lowest redshift source at z=0.139, is reminiscent of the so-called Little Red Dots found at high redshifts with JWST. Using the simple procedure followed here it is possible to locate previously undiscovered quasars with interesting properties, in particular Broad Absorption Line quasars and quasars with unusual extinction.
</p>

### 128) [2610.19598](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19598v1): Cosmology with strong gravitational lenses

<p> Tommaso Treu </p>
<p>
Three of the most pressing open questions in cosmology &ndash; the nature of dark energy and dark matter, and the growing tension in the value of the Hubble constant H_0 &ndash; call for cosmological probes that are independent of, and complementary to, traditional methods. Strong gravitational lensing offers exactly that: a purely gravitational probe of the universe's expansion history and matter distribution, sensitive to dark matter's presence regardless of whether it interacts with light. Time-delay lenses and multiplane lenses can measure H_0 and the dark energy equation of state directly, while the distortion of lensed images can reveal the abundance and internal structure of dark matter subhalos. After a brief theoretical and historical introduction, I review recent breakthroughs enabled by JWST and by advances in lens modeling and stellar dynamics. Examples include the recent measurement of H_0 with 3&percnt; precision in flat Λ cold dark matter cosmology, and a tentative detection of dark matter self-interaction. Strong lensing is entering a pivotal era: wide-field surveys with Euclid, Rubin, and Roman, and radio surveys with the Square Kilometer Array, are poised to increase the number of known strong lenses by orders of magnitude, positioning strong lensing to play a central role in answering these open questions in the decade ahead.
</p>

### 129) [2610.19379](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19379v1): Simulations of Dust Content and Attenuation in z=5-15 Galaxies

<p> Dhruv T. Zimmerman, Jed McKinney, Desika Narayanan, Caitlin M. Casey, John Chisholm, Alex M. Garcia, Michelle C. Jecmen, Jessica Kelley-Derzon, Federico Marinacci, Julian B. Muñoz, Laura V. Sales, Ethan Savitch, Paul Torrey </p>
<p>
Correctly modeling the dust attenuation law in galaxies is essential to properly inferring their growth and physical properties. With the rapid discovery of z&gt;6 galaxies by JWST and lack of constraints on the properties of dust at these redshifts, we must turn to theoretical models to predict the properties of dust at these redshifts. In this paper, we develop a theoretical foundation for the nature of high-redshift attenuation laws. We analyze 107 cosmological zoom-in simulations of galaxies run with the SMUGGLE galaxy formation model. SMUGGLE includes an explicitly modeled multiphase interstellar medium and a state-of-the-art dust model with a self-consistently evolving dust grain size distribution. We forward model these simulations with the radiative transfer package POWDERDAY to produce synthetic spectra and attenuation curves for a set of z=5-15, M_*~10^6.5-10^10 M<sub>&odot;</sub> galaxies. We find evidence for flatter attenuation curves than the commonly used locally-calibrated literature attenuation laws assumed when modeling high-redshift galaxies. The vast majority of our attenuation curves also lack the 2175 Angstrom absorption feature. Our attenuation laws do not evolve strongly over z=15-5, which we attribute to relatively constant fractions of small grains up to z=4 in our simulations. We present a set of simulation-informed priors on dust attenuation law parameterization that could be implemented in SED fitting codes. There is an anti-correlation between galaxy mass and attenuation curve slope and positive correlation between galaxy mass and A_V, which we attribute to dust growth becoming efficient in our massive galaxies with logM_*/M<sub>&odot;</sub>≳9 and with ISM conditions above a critical metallicity threshold.
</p>

### 130) [2610.19320](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19320v1): Large-scale Environmental Dependence of AGN Activity Revealed by JWST Paα Emitters

<p> Danyang Jiang, Linhua Jiang, Shuqi Fu, Zijian Zhang, Jie Chen, Chenxi Bao, Yihe Wang, Junya Arita, Darshan Kakkad, Jan-Torge Schindler </p>
<p>
The role of the large-scale environment in regulating AGN activity remains an open question. We investigate the Mpc-scale environments of 70 AGNs at z=1.1-1.5 using a highly complete sample of 3,419 Paα emitters with accurate spectroscopic redshifts from JWST slitless spectroscopic survey. This dataset allows us to robustly trace the environments and quantify the environmental dependence of AGN properties. We measure the eighth-nearest-neighbor surface density of Paα emitters around each AGN, probing ~1.5 pMpc scale environments. We find that this environmental density exhibits a weak positive correlation with radio luminosity. Radio-bright AGNs tend to reside in statistically denser environments than their radio-faint counterparts. The environmental density also has a positive correlation with the Eddington ratio and a weak negative correlation with the central black hole mass, implying more efficient black hole accretion in dense environments. We construct Paα luminosity functions (LFs) in AGN environments and find that they are systematically elevated relative to the field Paα LF. In addition, their faint-end slopes are shallower, suggesting accelerated galaxy growth and enhanced quenching of star formation. As Paα emitters trace gas-rich, star-forming galaxies, our results support a scenario in which the large-scale cold gas promotes AGN activity at cosmic noon.
</p>

### 131) [2610.19319](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19319v1): Hot Molecular Hydrogen in Galaxies at Redshift ~ 0.2-1.5 Revealed by JWST

<p> Jie Chen, Linhua Jiang, Shuqi Fu, Zijian Zhang, Danyang Jiang, Jing Wang, Ran Wang, Darshan Kakkad </p>
<p>
Molecular hydrogen (H<sub>2</sub>) is the fundamental component of the molecular gas reservoir that fuels star formation and black hole growth. While prominent hot H<sub>2</sub> emission (≳ 1000 K) has been explored in nearby galaxies, its excitation mechanisms at higher redshifts remain poorly constrained. We present a systematic study of hot molecular gas traced via ro-vibrational H<sub>2</sub> emitters at z~0.2-1.5 using JWST slitless spectroscopy. Exploiting JWST's great sensitivity to near-infrared H<sub>2</sub> ro-vibrational transitions such as H<sub>2</sub> 1-0 S(1), we build a sample of 144 H<sub>2</sub>-luminous galaxies and characterize their multi-wavelength properties. About 96.5% and 80% of these galaxies are detected in the radio and far-infrared (FIR) bands, respectively, and their hot H<sub>2</sub> emission strongly correlates with the radio and FIR radiation. Based on radio excess, X-ray emission, and optical diagnostics, 62 sources (43%) exhibit significant AGN activity. The H<sub>2</sub> line luminosities of the sample show a sublinear dependence on radio luminosity, while the relation between the H<sub>2</sub> luminosities and total infrared luminosities is approximately linear after excluding the radio-excess sources. These scaling relations suggest that star formation provides a baseline contribution to hot H<sub>2</sub> excitation, while radio-excess sources require an additional contribution from AGN-related processes such as jet- or wind-driven shocks. These results reveal a multi-channel excitation scenario and shed new light on the interplay between star formation, AGN activity, and the interstellar medium during galaxy evolution.
</p>

### 132) [2610.19318](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19318v1): A JWST View of Cosmic Star Formation from z~0 to 9 with Hydrogen Recombination Lines in the COSMOS and GOODS-N fields

<p> Shuqi Fu, Linhua Jiang, Christopher J. Conselice, Zijian Zhang, Danyang Jiang, Jie Chen, Louis Quilley, Fengwu Sun, Ronaldo Laishram, Alberto Traina, Bahareh S. Salmasi, Duncan Austin </p>
<p>
We combine wide-area COSMOS-3D observations with deeper GOODS-N data from JWST/NIRCam F444W wide-field slitless spectroscopy to trace cosmic star formation over z~0&ndash;9. The observations cover approximately 0.33~deg<sup>2</sup> in COSMOS and 60.9~arcmin<sup>2</sup> in GOODS-N, with typical 3σ integrated line-flux limits of 3.2×10^-18 and 1.04×10^-18~erg s<sup>-1</sup> cm<sup>2</sup>, respectively. The common 3.9&ndash;5.0 μm wavelength window probes Brα, Brβ, Paα, Paβ, Hα, and Hβ across the local Universe, cosmic noon, and reionization. Our final sample contains 9146 galaxies, including 7883 in COSMOS and 1263 in GOODS-N. COSMOS constrains luminous emitters, while GOODS-N extends the luminosity functions toward fainter sources. We measure dust-corrected line luminosities, account for line-detection completeness, and convert the six lines to equivalent Hα luminosities assuming Case B recombination. Schechter fits reveal an increase in the characteristic Hα-equivalent luminosity toward cosmic noon. Integrating the resulting star formation rate functions above 0.24 M<sub>&odot;</sub> yr^-1, we trace the cosmic star formation rate density from the local Universe to reionization. The combined Brackett, Paschen, and Balmer measurements are broadly consistent with the canonical UV- and IR-based cosmic star formation history at z≲6. Fixed-slope tests show that the Hβ estimate at z~8 remains sensitive to the external photometric faint-end constraint and extrapolation below our spectroscopic limit. These measurements provide complementary constraints on the distribution and integrated rate of star formation across cosmic time.
</p>

### 133) [2610.19244](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19244v1): Tracing Accretion and Inner-Disk Gas Evolution in the Orion Nebula via Br-alpha and CO

<p> Konstantin V. Getman, Maria Claudia Ramirez-Tannus, Jenny Frediani, Kevin L. Luhman, Eric D. Feigelson, Bayron Portilla-Revelo, Christian Rab, Valentin Brunn, Jan Forbrich, Thomas J. Haworth, Rens Waters, Arjan Bik, Charles J. Law, Andrew J. Winter, Vladimir S. Airapetian, Abygail R. Waggoner, Michael A. Kuhn, L. Ilsedore Cleeves, Sergio A. Dzib, Marco Padovani </p>
<p>
We present a systematic spectroscopic study of hydrogen Brackett-alpha (Br-alpha) and carbon monoxide (CO) fundamental-band (4.3-5.2 micron) emission from a homogeneous sample of 0.2-1.5 M<sub>&odot;</sub> pre-main-sequence (PMS) stars in the Orion Nebula observed with JWST/NIRSpec-MOS as part of our Chandra-JWST project. Using 73 Br-alpha and 46 CO spectra, we investigate correlations between warm-gas emission, stellar properties, accretion, and environment with censored correlation and regression analyses accounting for multiple testing. Br-alpha luminosity correlates strongly with accretion luminosity (Lacc), even after excluding sources within 2' of Theta1 Ori C, the nebula's main ionizing O-type star, whose radiation may bias proplyd Lacc estimates. This supports Br-alpha as a robust empirical tracer of accretion in PMS stars. Br-alpha also correlates with visual extinction (Av) and more weakly with stellar bolometric luminosity and mass. CO fundamental-band luminosities likewise correlate positively with Lacc and Av, relationships that persist in a larger combined JWST/NIRSpec+MIRI sample spanning multiple star-forming regions. In contrast to Br-alpha, CO luminosity shows no significant dependence on basic stellar properties, suggesting that warm molecular gas is governed primarily by accretion and local disk conditions. The weaker correlations of the Spitzer/IRAC spectral slope with Lacc and Av suggest that warm inner-disk gas may evolve or disperse faster than optically thick dust. Overall, the CO results tentatively indicate declining warm-gas emission as disks evolve, modulated by accretion-powered thermal and ultraviolet excitation, although variations in emitting area inherited from natal core conditions may also contribute.
</p>

### 134) [2610.19182](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19182v2): Galactic Outflows and Star-Formation Quenching: A JWST Perspective

<p> Rebecca L. Davies, Sirio Belli </p>
<p>
Outflows driven by active galactic nuclei (AGN) are widely considered a key mechanism for regulating and quenching star formation in massive galaxies. However, direct observational evidence linking outflows to quenching has remained elusive for decades. This article reviews recent findings for massive galaxies at cosmic noon (z~ 2), based on sensitive James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) observations tracing both star formation histories (through Balmer absorption lines) and neutral outflows (through blueshifted NaID absorption). Neutral outflows are found in ~ 25 - 50% of massive (log(M_*/M<sub>&odot;</sub>)&gt; 10) galaxies, independent of star formation rate. Outflows from star-forming galaxies are consistent with being powered by star-formation and/or AGN activity, whereas outflows from quiescent systems are generally attributed to current or previous AGN activity. There is growing evidence that recent rapid quenching of star-formation is associated with powerful AGN-driven outflows with mass loading factors η≳ 1 - 10. The neutral gas dominates the outflow mass budget but in most cases its velocity is below the halo escape velocity, indicating that this gas may form part of a galactic fountain. Detections of neutral gas inflows and residual cold gas reservoirs in quiescent galaxies suggest that other feedback mechanisms such as AGN heating are required to keep galaxies quenched over long timescales. We highlight the potential of multiphase outflow measurements to constrain feedback models, describe the main systematic uncertainties in mass outflow rate measurements, and discuss how the z~ 2 results inform our understanding of the formation of the earliest massive quiescent galaxies.
</p>

### 135) [2610.18998](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18998v1): The convective-transition gap in 47 Tucanae with JWST

<p> M. Scalco, L. R. Bedin, M. Libralato, M. Griggio, E. Vesperini, R. Gerasimov </p>
<p>
We report the detection of the convective-transition gap along the lower main sequence (MS) of the Galactic globular cluster (GC) 47 Tucanae using deep James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) observations. We analyse the stellar luminosity function (LF) in the vicinity of the gap location using two independent colour-magnitude diagrams (CMDs). In both cases, the LF is characterised by a narrow gap followed by a discontinuous increase in the stellar number counts, and presents an overall morphology qualitatively consistent with that reported in previous studies. By modelling the observed LFs with an empirical likelihood model, we estimate the statistical significance of the observed feature. Additional JWST passbands exhibit similar signatures, providing further evidence for the presence of a discontinuity.

### 136) [2610.18996](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18996v1): The mass functions of multiple stellar populations in 47 Tucanae

<p> M. Scalco, J. Bruce, E. Vesperini, L. R. Bedin </p>
<p>
We used a combination of James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) and Hubble Space Telescope (HST) observations to investigate the mass functions (MFs) of the different stellar populations in the globular cluster (GC) 47 Tucanae (NGC 104) over a wide stellar-mass interval. We identified the multiple populations along the lower main sequence using only JWST photometry and, in particular, colour combinations sensitive to variations in oxygen abundance. We derived the MFs of the first- and second-population (1P and 2P) stars separately and found that in the low-mass regime, the present-day MF of the 1P population is significantly steeper than that of the 2P population, which appears nearly flat. We also present the results of a dynamical model showing that this behaviour requires a different initial mass function (IMF) for the 2P and the 1P populations in the 0.1-0.4 solar masses mass range, thus providing a new fundamental constraint for the study of the star formation of multiple populations in GCs.

### 137) [2610.18995](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18995v1): JWST observations of 47 Tucanae: I. The low-mass main sequence down to the hydrogen-burning limit

<p> M. Scalco, R. Gerasimov, L. R. Bedin, E. Vesperini, M. Griggio, D. Nardiello, J. Anderson </p>
<p>
We present the first results of a series of studies based on JWST observations of the globular cluster 47 Tucanae (47 Tuc, or NGC 104) obtained with NIRCam. By combining new data with earlier JWST observations, we derive high-precision proper motions (PMs) over a multi-year baseline (of ~3 years) and construct a clean sample of cluster members extending to very faint magnitudes (F444W~24.25 mag, corresponding to ~0.077 solar masses, slightly above the estimated hydrogen-burning limit of 47 Tuc, ~0.074 solar masses) along the main sequence (MS). We investigated the presence of the low-mass MS kink previously identified in 47 Tuc using the new F090W and F444W photometry. No significant signature of the feature is found using these filters, consistent with its proposed physical interpretation as a consequence of the transition in carbon chemistry toward a methane-dominated equilibrium in very low-mass stars. A comparison with theoretical isochrones indicates that the stellar population near the bottom of the MS is likely dominated by oxygen-poor stars, in agreement with previous findings. Using artificial-star tests, we quantify the completeness of our sample and show that the requirement of PM-based membership significantly limits the detection of the faintest objects, indicating that current JWST observations of 47 Tuc are not sufficiently deep to reliably trace the cluster's brown dwarf cooling sequence. The mass function (MF) of 47 Tuc shows a structured behaviour, with a break at ~0.15 solar masses not associated with the onset of convection, and a mild but systematic radial variation consistent with mass segregation. Our analysis indicates that the MF shape in this mass regime is more complex than a simple power-law description. This feature in the MF may point to a presently unrecognised mechanism affecting the formation or dynamical evolution of low-mass stars in globular clusters.
</p>

### 138) [2610.18960](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18960v1): How JWST is Revolutionizing Our View of Early Galaxy Formation

<p> Rychard Bouwens </p>
<p>
The James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) has been revolutionizing our understanding of early galaxy formation over its first four years of science operations. With un- precedented sensitivity, spatial resolution, and wavelength coverage from the near- to mid-infrared, JWST has enabled astronomers to identify hundreds of star-forming galaxies at redshifts z&gt;~8 (within ~650 million years of the Big Bang) through strong spectral breaks and characterize these galaxies through sensitive spectroscopy. These capabilities have provided new insights into the stellar populations, neutral gas, nebular continuum emission, chemical enrichment, dust content, and ionization conditions of galaxies during cosmic dawn, revealing more numerous and brighter systems than anticipated from pre-JWST models. We discuss the implications of these discoveries for reionization, the buildup of stellar mass and metals, and the evolution of galaxy populations and luminosity functions. JWST is also revealing a new physical picture of early galaxies, characterized by compact, clumpy star formation, high gas fractions, and intense radiation fields. Despite much progress, many open questions remain, including the efficiency, intermittency, and stellar initial mass function (IMF) of early star formation, conditions in the interstellar medium (ISM), masses, and structure of the earliest galaxies, and the topology of reionization. Resolving these open questions will require lensing magnification, sensitive spectroscopy to &gt; 5 micron, including with the Atacama Large Millimeter Array (ALMA), exploration to higher redshifts, and larger, more complete samples, helping to clarify how the first galaxies formed and evolved.
</p>

### 139) [2610.18263](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18263v1): Massive black holes in the first billion years with JWST

<p> Hannah Übler </p>
<p>
The James Webb Space Telescope has unlocked uncharted territory for the study of early black holes, enabling the detection of fainter and smaller black holes at larger cosmological distances than previously possible. Compared to nearby massive black holes, they are characterised by several unexpected attributes, such as their multi-wavelength properties or their relation to their host galaxies. These observational findings challenge our understanding of the past evolution of present-day supermassive black holes, and provide novel anchor points to constrain theoretical models of black hole formation and growth. This review summarises our current understanding of the observed properties of newly discovered, active black holes in the early Universe, discusses the status of mass measurements and the relation to their hosts, as well as open questions and future prospects.
</p>

### 140) [2610.17575](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17575v1): Mergers may not be the main channel for galaxy stellar mass growth at redshift z≳5: Predictions from cosmological hierarchical models and simulations

<p> Hao Fu, Mohammadreza Ayromlou, Francesco Shankar, Lars Hernquist, Andrea Lapi, Daniel Roberts, Feng Yuan, Maulik Bhatt, Evgenii Chaikin, Simona Gallerani, Luca Graziani, Antonio La Marca, George Mountrichas, Yingjie Peng, Haocheng Wang, Kai Wang </p>
<p>
Mergers are a crucial channel of galaxy growth, but their role relative to star formation remains uncertain at high redshift. Here we investigate merger rates and merger-driven stellar mass assembly for galaxies with stellar mass M<sub>&sext;</sub> ≳ 10^8   M<sub>&odot;</sub> at z≃ 5-11, by combining complementary theoretical approaches within ΛCDM. These include the DECODE semi-empirical model, the TNG100 and EAGLE hydrodynamical simulations, and the scL-Galaxies semi-analytical model, together with the Millennium and TNG100-Dark simulations used to examine dark matter subhalo statistics and merger timescales. This compares models with different treatments of baryonic physics and galaxy-halo connections. We find that dark matter-only simulations predict merger timescales ~ 30&percnt; longer than those inferred from cosmological simulations including baryons. Galaxy major merger (M<sub>&sext;</sub>,sat / M<sub>&sext;</sub>,cen &gt; 0.25) rates are high at cosmic dawn and increase towards earlier epochs, reaching ~ 1/Gyr at z~ 5 and peaking at ~ 3/Gyr at z~ 8. Despite these high rates, mergers contribute only ≲ 10&percnt; of the total cosmic stellar mass density evolution. The ex-situ stellar mass fraction ranges from a few percent to ~ 20&percnt; for all galaxies at z≳ 5, across different models and simulations. Their broad agreement implies that stellar mass assembly at cosmic dawn is dominated by in-situ star formation rather than mergers. These findings suggest that the high pair fractions identified by JWST may not directly trace efficient ex-situ stellar mass growth, highlighting the need for physically motivated merger timescales to interpret galaxy assembly in the early Universe.
</p>

### 141) [2610.17081](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17081v1): Extinction by non-spherical particles with the Nanoparticle Transition Matrix Code: perspectives for interstellar medium and exoplanetary atmospheres

<p> G. La Mura, G. Mulas, M. A. Iatì, C. Cecchi-Pestellini, G. Aresu, S. Rezaei, R. Saija </p>
<p>
The Transition Matrix formalism (T-matrix) offers an optimal solution to the problem of radiation scattering and absorption by particles characterized by a high degree of chemical and structural complexity. However, its application to particle models composed by large numbers of constituents has been classically limited by the substantial computational burden of the method. As a consequence, the investigation of light scattering and absorption processes, in cases where particle asymmetry and non-homogeneity play an important role, typically need to rely on simplifying assumptions which affect the correct interpretation of transmission spectra and extinction curves. This problem has a huge impact on many fields of scientific application. In particular, it has a critical role in astrophysics, because the ability to model highly non homogeneous particles is a fundamental requirement to understand the role of extinction in different phases of the interstellar medium (ISM), as well as to probe the physics of light transmission in planetary atmospheres within the Solar System and beyond. In this work we describe the application of a new implementation of the T-matrix formalism, based on the framework originally developed by Borghese, Denti & Saija and named Nano-particle Transition Matrix code, which leverages the power of modern hardware architectures to reduce the computational costs related to the solution of relevant particle models for the investigation of dust in the ISM and of hot-Jupyter class planets atmospheric aerosols. This new implementation of the T-matrix formalism can handle calculations of increasing complexity with a direct impact in the interpretation of the transmission spectra of transiting exoplanets produced by the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) and of those expected from the Atmospheric Remote-sensing Infrared Exoplanet Large survey (ARIEL).
</p>

### 142) [2610.15901](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15901v1): The DAWN JWST Archive: A spectroscopically-informed population study of the physical properties of galaxies at z&gt;2

<p> Rashmi Gottumukkala, Gabriel Brammer, Sune Toft, Mengyuan Xiao, Anna de Graaff, Pascal A. Oesch, Francesco Valentino, Katherine E. Whitaker, William M. Baker, Laia Barrufet, Rachel Bezanson, Leindert A. Boogaard, Alex J. Cameron, Olivia R. Cooper, Emma Giovinazzo, Kasper E. Heintz, Michaela Hirschmann, Kei Ito, Ivo Labbe, Minju M. Lee, Zach J. Lewis, Jorryt Matthee, Ian McConachie, Desika Narayanan, Clara L. Pollock, Sedona H. Price, David J. Setton, Marko Shuntov, Pieter van Dokkum, Bingjie Wang, Joris Witstok, Yunchong Zhang, Pengpei Zhu </p>
<p>
Charting the evolution of star-formation, stellar mass and dust build-up over cosmic time relies on the accurate characterisation of the stellar population properties of high redshift galaxies. We present a catalogue of spectroscopically derived physical properties for ~12,000 galaxies at z&gt;2, with archival JWST/NIRSpec PRISM spectroscopy and JWST/NIRCam imaging from the DAWN JWST Archive. We present a custom version of the SED fitting code BAGPIPES, used to perform joint spectrophotometric modelling of PRISM spectra and broadband NIRCam photometry. In doing so, we publicly release a science-ready catalogue of the physical properties of galaxies beyond cosmic noon. We report key physical properties, such as stellar masses, star-formation rates (SFR) and timescales, and V-band dust attenuation (Av). We further probe the recent star-formation histories (SFH) of galaxies via their SFRs on 3 Myr, 10 Myr, and 100 Myr timescales, capturing both rising and falling recent SFHs. Through dust-corrected ugi colour-colour diagrams of massive galaxies, we show how quiescent and dusty galaxies populate this colour space at z&gt;2, with galaxies experiencing a recent downturn in star-formation occupying distinct parameter space. We also study the dustiest galaxies in our sample, showing that they are preferentially more massive and strongly star-forming relative to the parent sample, residing on the high-mass end of the star-forming main-sequence. The slope of the attenuation curve has a strong dependence on Av, varying from steep to shallow as the dust attenuation increases and the specific star-formation rate decreases. Our findings show that the unparalleled number statistics of this catalogue of spectroscopically derived stellar population properties uniquely enables the study star-formation, quenching and dust properties of galaxies from cosmic noon to cosmic dawn.
</p>

### 143) [2610.15255](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15255v1): Anisotropic Radiation in Super-Eddington Active Galactic Nuclei: The Responsivity of Broad-Line Regions to Continuum Variability

<p> Feng-Na Fang, Pu Du, Jian-Min Wang </p>
<p>
Anisotropic ionizing radiation is a natural theoretical expectation for super-Eddington accretion in active galactic nuclei (AGNs), implying that the ionizing flux incident on the broad-line region (BLR) clouds differs from that measured along the observer's line of sight. Reverberation mapping studies have revealed systematically shortened time lags of the broad Hβ emission line relative to continuum variations in super-Eddington AGNs, a phenomenon that can be naturally interpreted as a consequence of anisotropic ionizing continuum radiation. To further investigate this anisotropy, we perform a systematic analysis of the Hβ line responsivity, defined as the ratio of the variability amplitude of Hβ to that of the driving continuum, for both sub- and super-Eddington AGNs. By combining the effective responsivities measured relative to the driving continuum, after correcting for the continuum&ndash;emission-line delay, with photoionization calculations, we infer the ionizing radiation field received by their BLRs. We find that, in super-Eddington AGNs, the ionizing flux inferred from the Hβ responsivity differs significantly from that observed along the line of sight, whereas the two are in closer agreement in sub-Eddington AGNs. These results provide compelling evidence for strong anisotropy of the incident ionizing radiation in super-Eddington AGNs. We conclude by discussing the broader implications of this anisotropy, including its potential relevance to the "little red dots" recently discovered by the James Webb Space Telescope.
</p>

### 144) [2610.14881](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14881v2): TDCOSMO 2026: new insights on the Hubble tension from strong lensing time delays

<p> TDCOSMO Collaboration, Simon Birrer, Michele Cappellari, Frédéric Courbin, Christopher D. Fassnacht, Pierre Fleury, Joshua A. Frieman, Aymeric Galan, Xiang-Yu Huang, Daniel P. Johnson, Sanjana Khanolkar, Shawn Knabel, Hrvoje Krizic, Martin Millon, Takahiro Morishita, Veronica Motta, Pritom Mozumdar, Eric Paic, Hadrien Paugnat, Allan G. Schweinfurth, Anowar J. Shajib, William Sheu, Dominique Sluse, Massimo Stiavelli, Sherry H. Suyu, Tommaso Treu, Felipe Urcelay, Devon M. Williams, Kenneth C. Wong </p>
<p>
We discuss the measurement of the Hubble constant (H_0) in flat Λ cold dark matter cosmology based on the blind hierarchical analysis of 11 multiply-imaged quasars (hereafter the TDCOSMO-2026 sample) and described in greater detail in a companion paper. In addition to the time delays, we use lens models based on high-resolution imaging from the Hubble Space Telescope (HST) and from the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST), analyzed jointly with axisymmetric dynamical models based on JWST-NIRSpec, VLT-MUSE, and Keck-KCWI spectroscopy. Our primary result H_0=71.1^+2.4_-2.3 km.s<sup>-1</sup>.Mpc^-1 is based on the TDCOSMO-2026 sample, in combination with Ω_m constraints from the DES-Dovekie supernova (SN) dataset. Our 3% precision accounts for the mass-sheet degeneracy. Importantly, our measurement is independent of the local distance ladder (LDL). Among individual late-universe probes, only the traditional LDL is more precise. We discuss our result in the context of the Hubble tension, the difference between H_0 measured in the late universe and the prediction based on early-universe probes such as the cosmic microwave background (CMB). We construct combinations of independent late-universe probes and show that the tension with the Planck CMB prediction is robust to excluding any one of the methods based on Type-Ia Supernovae, Cepheids, or the Tip of the Red Giant Branch. For example, we construct a 'no-SN' H_0 measurement, by replacing the DES-Dovekie constraint on Ω_m with that from DESI baryon acoustic oscillations, and then combining TDCOSMO-2026 with independent non-SN late-universe probes. This 'no-SN' late-universe probe is in 3.7σ tension with Planck. Even after completely excluding the LDL, the tension with Planck persists at the 2.7σ level.
</p>

### 145) [2610.14781](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14781v2): TDCOSMO 2026: A 3% measurement of the Hubble constant from strong lensing and stellar kinematics

<p> TDCOSMO Collaboration, Simon Birrer, Michele Cappellari, Frédéric Courbin, Christopher D. Fassnacht, Pierre Fleury, Joshua A. Frieman, Aymeric Galan, Xiang-Yu Huang, Daniel P. Johnson, Sanjana Khanolkar, Shawn Knabel, Hrvoje Krizic, Martin Millon, Takahiro Morishita, Veronica Motta, Pritom Mozumdar, Eric Paic, Hadrien Paugnat, Allan G. Schweinfurth, Anowar J. Shajib, William Sheu, Dominique Sluse, Massimo Stiavelli, Sherry H. Suyu, Tommaso Treu, Felipe Urcelay, Devon M. Williams, Kenneth C. Wong </p>
<p>
We present cosmological constraints from 11 strongly lensed quasars with measured time delays (hereafter, the TDCOSMO-2026 sample). In addition to expanding the sample, we improve many aspects of our blind analysis with respect to our 2025 analysis. Among other improvements, spatially resolved kinematics are available for nine systems, including six with JWST-NIRSpec, compared to one in 2025; for three lens models, JWST-NIRCam images now replace HST; axisymmetric dynamical modeling is applied directly to the spatially resolved data, instead of an axisymmetric correction factor applied to spherical models; and the line-of-sight convergence is now based on the Euclid Flagship simulations instead of the Millennium Simulation. Our baseline and primary result is based on the TDCOSMO-2026 sample, in combination with a prior on Ω_m from the DES-Dovekie supernova sample. In the flat Λ cold dark matter (ΛCDM) cosmology, we find H_0=71.1^+2.4_-2.3 km.s<sup>-1</sup>.Mpc^-1, a 3.3% uncertainty that accounts for the mass-sheet degeneracy. The SLACS and SL2S samples are in excellent agreement with the TDCOSMO-2026 sample, improving the precision on H_0 in flat ΛCDM to 3.0%. Using the DESI-DR2 baryon acoustic oscillations (BAO) likelihood instead of DES-Dovekie yields fully consistent results. We also present constraints in the open ΛCDM, wCDM, w_0w_aCDM, and w_φCDM cosmologies; the TDCOSMO H_0 inference is robust and consistent across all of them. The combination of the TDCOSMO+SLACS+SL2S likelihood with those from DESI-DR2 and DES-Dovekie disfavors w=-1 at the 2.3-2.5σ level in all three dark energy extensions that we tested. We find, e.g., w_0=-0.88^+0.04_-0.05 in w_φCDM and w_0 = -0.85^+0.07_-0.06 in w_0w_aCDM, while w_a = -0.48^+0.46_-0.44 remains consistent with zero.

### 146) [2610.14653](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14653v1): Multiphase Analysis of (U)LIRG Nuclear Activity (MAUNA). I. Spatially Resolved Optical Emission-Line Diagnostics

<p> Justin A. Kader, Vivian U, Marina Bianchin, Yiqing Song, George C. Privon, Sabrina Stierwalt, Tanio Diaz-Santos, Lee Armus, Vassilis Charmandaris, Ezequiel Treister, Rosalie McGurk, Hanae Inami, Jeffrey A. Rich, Sean T. Linden, Anne M. Medling, Loreto Barcos-Muñoz, Gabriela Canalizo, Aaron S. Evans, Kirsten L. Larson, Tianmu Gao, Thomas Lai, Laura Lenkic, Jaya Nagarajan-Swenson, Justin Howell, Cristina M. Lofaro, Matthew A. Malkan, María Sánchez-García, Christopher D. Martin, Mateusz Matuszewski, Claire E. Max, Joseph M. Mazzarella, James D. Neill, Nikolaus Z. Prusinski, Raymond Remigio, David Sanders, Jason Surace </p>
<p>
We present resolved optical integral-field spectroscopy of the 32 luminous and ultraluminous infrared galaxy systems of the Multiphase Analysis of (U)LIRG Nuclear Activity (MAUNA) sample, drawn from the GOALS survey and selected to have JWST/MIRI medium-resolution spectrometer (MRS) observations. The data set combines new Keck/KCWI observations of 10 systems with archival VLT/MUSE cubes for 22 systems, covering Hβ to [S II] at ~1 arcsecond spatial resolution (~0.9 kpc at the sample median distance). Using the optical [N II]/Hα BPT diagram, we find that composite and LINER-like excitation together account for most of the classified area (median fractions 0.44 and 0.26), while Seyfert-like gas covers a median fraction of 0.02 but carries a far larger proportion of the line luminosity where it is present. Both the projected size of the Seyfert-classified region, R_Sy=0.08-7.5 kpc, and its [O III] luminosity increase with the bolometric AGN luminosity, with R_Sy&prop; L_AGN,bol^0.54±0.18, as expected for gas photoionized at a constant ionization parameter. The scatter about the luminosity relation depends on dust opacity: the [O III] luminosity per unit AGN luminosity falls by 1.09±0.25 dex per unit depth of the 9.7 μm silicate absorption feature (Spearman ρ=-0.60, p=0.001). Optical excitation diagnostics therefore locate AGN-excited gas reliably but systematically underestimate the luminosity the AGN supplies to the host in the most obscured (U)LIRGs.
</p>

### 147) [2610.13920](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13920v1): A Comprehensive JWST Slitless Spectroscopic Census of ~12,000 Emission-Line Galaxies at Redshift 0~9 in COSMOS

<p> Shuqi Fu, Zijian Zhang, Danyang Jiang, Jie Chen, Linhua Jiang, Chenxi Bao, Yihe Wang, Ronaldo Laishram </p>
<p>
Emission-line galaxies provide key diagnostics of star formation, chemical enrichment, and AGN activity across cosmic time. We present a systematic census of emission-line sources using JWST/NIRCam wide-field slitless spectroscopy (WFSS) from the COSMOS-3D program, which covers a large contiguous area of 0.33 deg<sup>2</sup> and provides F444W grism spectroscopy over 3.9&ndash;5.1 μm. By combining slitless spectroscopy with deep multi-band imaging data, we construct a homogeneous spectroscopic sample of 12,275 objects at redshift 0&ndash;9. The typical 3σ line-flux limit reaches 3.2×10^-18 erg s<sup>-1</sup> cm<sup>2</sup>. To address source confusion inherent to slitless spectroscopy, we develop a multi-step identification framework that combines information from spectra, images, spectral energy distributions, and spatial distributions. We introduce convolutional neural network and large language model to emulate the human identification process and improve efficiency. The resulting catalog provides spectroscopic redshifts, emission-line measurements, and derived physical properties for all sources. The F444W WFSS enables the detection of multiple emission lines, including primarily [O III], Hα, He I &lambda;10830, [S III] λ&lambda;9531,9069, Paβ, Paα, Brβ, PAH, Brα, allowing a continuous census of galaxies across cosmic time. We construct composite spectra in several redshift bins and detect a variety of faint spectral features that are difficult to identify in individual spectra, including higher-order hydrogen recombination lines, CO absorption bands, and [Fe II] lines. This catalog provides a key reference dataset for studies of galaxy evolution and AGN activity, and demonstrates the power of WFSS for constructing large, unbiased spectroscopic samples over wide areas. The full catalog will be publicly available online upon publication.
</p>

### 148) [2610.13758](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13758v1): A dust-reddened quasar in a [C II]-luminous host at z=6.55

<p> Daming Yang, Joseph F. Hennawi, Rychard Bouwens, Timo Kist, Eduardo Bañados, Hiddo S. B. Algera, Aaron J. Barth, Sarah E. I. Bosman, Frederick B. Davies, Anna-Christina Eilers, Xiaohui Fan, Victoria A. Fawcett, Yuming Fu, Anniek J. Gloudemans, Francesco Guarneri, Bin Jia, Elia Pizzati, Huub Röttgering, Jan-Torge Schindler, Feige Wang, Jinyi Yang </p>
<p>
Most known quasars at z&gt;6 were selected through their rest-frame UV emission. Only a small number of reddened quasars have been identified at these redshifts, leaving their nuclear and host-galaxy properties poorly constrained. We present JWST/NIRSpec spectroscopy and ALMA band 6 observations of the quasar J1440+0019 at z=6.5488, revealing its dust-reddened nature and jointly characterizing its nucleus and host galaxy. The continuum is best described by an SMC-like extinction law with E(B-V)=0.20±0.03. Three absorption systems detected in N V, Si IV, and C IV are blueshifted by approximately 900&ndash;3600 km s<sup>-1</sup> and likely trace an ionized nuclear outflow. The broad Mg II line and extinction-corrected continuum yield a black-hole mass of log(M_BH/M<sub>&odot;</sub>)=9.04±0.42, a bolometric luminosity of L_bol≃1.1×10^47 erg s<sup>-1</sup>, and an Eddington ratio of λ_Edd≃0.8. ALMA observations further reveal its host galaxy to be among the most [C II]-luminous known at z&gt;6, with L_[C II]=(7.21±0.25)×10^9 L<sub>&odot;</sub>. For a fiducial dust temperature of 47 K and emissivity index β=1.6, the dust continuum implies L_TIR=8.9×10^12 L<sub>&odot;</sub> and M_dust=4.0×10^8 M<sub>&odot;</sub>. The inferred dynamical mass places J1440+0019 in the region of the M_BH&ndash;M_dyn plane occupied by high-redshift blue quasars. These observations reveal a moderately reddened, rapidly accreting quasar in a luminous, dust-rich host during reionization. Its measured nuclear and host-galaxy properties provide an observational reference for identifying and characterizing similar systems in future systematic searches with wide-area infrared surveys.
</p>

### 149) [2610.12591](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12591v1): When dust growth takes over: the origin of dust in post-reionization galaxies

<p> Omima Osman, Gabriella De Lucia, Andreas. L. Faisst, Lizhi Xie, Fabio Fontanot, Michaela Hirschmann </p>
<p>
The post-reionization epoch (z~4-6) marks a critical phase in galaxy evolution, during which galaxies rapidly assemble their stellar mass, enrich their interstellar medium, and build up substantial dust reservoirs. The physical processes responsible for such rapid dust enrichment remain uncertain. We investigate the dust enrichment history of the ALPINE-CRISTAL-JWST (ACJ) sample using the semi-analytic galaxy formation model GAlaxy Evolution and Assembly (GAEA), which follows galaxy assembly, chemical enrichment, and dust evolution. We select model galaxies matching the observed stellar masses, star formation rates, and metallicities of the ACJ sample, compare their dust properties with observed scaling relations, and trace their enrichment histories and environments. The model reproduces the observed dust mass-stellar mass relation and the dust-to-stellar mass, dust-to-gas, and dust-to-metal ratios as functions of stellar mass and metallicity. Reproducing these constraints requires efficient dust growth in the interstellar medium; stellar dust production alone cannot account for the observed dust reservoirs, even under the most optimistic assumptions. The combination of stellar mass, star formation rate, and metallicity provides a good predictor of galaxy dust content. Our model suggests rapid dust build-up consistent with the previously noted rapid metal enrichment, with evidence for a transition around z~8-9 below which dust growth becomes the dominant dust formation channel. Finally, ACJ-like galaxies preferentially reside in locally overdense environments, suggesting that environment may have contributed to their accelerated evolution. Taken together, these results support rapid and coupled stellar, metal, and dust build-up in ACJ galaxies during the post-reionization era.
</p>

### 150) [2610.12577](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12577v1): The road to H_0, Ω_m, and w with a global neutrino-telescope network

<p> Pavel Zhelnin, Gonzalo Herrera, Nicholas Kamp, Carlos A. Argüelles </p>
<p>
The newly measured correlation between the neutrino and X-ray luminosities of Seyfert galaxies has yielded the first neutrino-based measurement of the Hubble constant. Building on this result, we forecast the reach of a global neutrino-telescope network by 2030, utilizing full-sky IceCube exposures combined with KM3NeT-ARCA and Baikal-GVD. We find that this 2030 configuration sharpens the neutrino-measured Hubble constant from today's H_0 = 66^+44_-37 to H_0 =66^+15_-13 km s<sup>-1</sup> Mpc^-1. Additionally, we project that the disk-corona slope of the neutrino X-ray luminosity relation (L_ν&prop; L_X^β) will reach β=0.72±0.04, enabling a ≥ \!5σ exclusion of the calorimetric value β=1. Finally, we show that, if a high-redshift population of neutrino sources, such as JWST's "little red dots," is identified and obeys the same luminosity relation, we can obtain a new independent measurement of the matter density Ω_m and the dark energy equation-of-state parameter w.
</p>

### 151) [2610.12553](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12553v1): A Single Line-Emitter Identified in an Unbiased Search with JWST/NIRSpec: Multi-Object Coronagraphy for Slitless Spectroscopy

<p> Mason S. Huberty, Claudia Scarlata, Matthew J. Hayes, Alaina Henry, Danielle A. Berg, Vieri Cammelli, Richard Ellis, Anne E. Jaskot, Yu-Heng Lin, Vihang Mehta, Matilde Mingozzi, Pierluigi Monaco, Alberto Saldana-Lopez, Aayush Saxena, Jonathan C. Tan, Alice Young </p>
<p>
We report the detection of a previously unknown object with a single emission line within the Hubble Ultra Deep Field, using a new JWST NIRSpec technique called Multi-Object Coronagraphy for Slitless Spectroscopy (MOCSS) with the G140M/F100LP observing mode. The MOCSS technique involves opening almost all the shutters on the NIRSpec microshutter array, while simultaneously closing the shutters that lie on top of known low redshift objects, facilitating a wide field slitless spectroscopic search without contamination from bright objects. This observational technique has enabled the detection of a strong emission line in four different dither positions and in two position angles, and thus cannot be artificial. This line is detected at 1.60 μm with a line flux of [2.5± 0.3]×10^-18 erg s<sup>-1</sup> cm<sup>2</sup> and a FWHM of 13.2±2.0Å, and does not correspond to any known source with some of the deepest HST and JWST imaging in existence. Based upon the absence of imaging detection, we place a lower limit on the observed frame equivalent width (EW) of 7620Å on this object, which strongly limits the number of possible lines that this line can be. Given the wavelength, an EW limit that is near the ceiling of expectation, and the lack of other lines in the bandpass, this line cannot be easily explained by [OII] at z=3.29, [OIII] at z=2.20, Hα at z=1.44, and Lyα at z=12.16. We discuss possible AGN and transient phenomena that could explain the finding.
</p>

### 152) [2610.12398](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12398v2): ACORN I. Massive Black Hole Seeding and Tidal Disruption Events from Star Clusters in Cosmological Simulations

<p> Yihao Zhou, Tiziana Di Matteo, Claire E. Williams, Aklant Kumar Bhowmick, Marta Reina-Campos, Carl L. Rodriguez, Elena González Prieto, Laura Blecha, Rupert Croft </p>
<p>
JWST has revealed massive star clusters at z≳ 6, which provide promising environments for massive black hole (MBH) seed formation. We introduce a new MBH seeding model for cosmological simulations, physically motivated by runaway collisions in star clusters. Subgrid star clusters are generated according to the local ISM and evolved through stellar evolution and two-body relaxation. The masses of MBH seeds formed through runaway collisions are determined by the properties of their host clusters, including the effects of metallicity-dependent stellar winds. We further follow tidal disruption events (TDEs) with the evolving clusters, which can be enhanced in MBH binaries, and account for gravitational-wave (GW) recoil following MBH mergers. We implement this model in a constrained simulation of a 5σ overdense region, where seeds of 10^3-10^5.6 M<sub>&odot;</sub> form in clusters with 10^4≤ m_SC≤ 10^8 M<sub>&odot;</sub>. Because both the seed abundance and mass are directly connected to the local environment, we have a more physical M_BH\!-\!M_galaxy scaling for low-mass galaxies (M_galaxy~10^6-9 M<sub>&odot;</sub>), a regime poorly described by previous seeding models. Multiple MBHs are naturally allowed to form in-situ within individual halos, producing a spatially clustered population. This increases the MBH merger rate by ~100 relative to other seeding models, significantly enhancing the expected LISA event rate. TDEs can dominate the growth of 10^3-4 M<sub>&odot;</sub> MBHs and produce luminous flares of L_peak~ 10^42-43 erg s<sup>-1</sup>, increasing the observability of low-mass seeds. This seeding prescription accelerates early MBH growth at z≳ 8, while subsequent self-regulated gas accretion drives the central MBH toward similar masses ~10^9  M<sub>&odot;</sub> by z=6 across different seeding models.
</p>

### 153) [2610.12322](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12322v1): JWST CAPERS: Spectroscopic evaluation of very high redshift galaxy candidates

<p> Callum T. Donnan, Mark Dickinson, Lorenzo Napolitano, Pablo Arrabal Haro, Anthony J. Taylor, Hollis B. Akins, Ryan Begley, Davide Bevacqua, Adam C. Carnall, Marco Castellano, Fergus Cullen, James S. Dunlop, Steven L. Finkelstein, Carter Flayhart, Mauro Giavalisco, Michaela Hirschmann, Ray A. Lucas, Derek J. McLeod, Ross J. McLure, Casey Papovich, Laura Pentericci, Borja Pérez-Díaz, Thomas M. Stanton, L. Y. Aaron Yung </p>
<p>
JWST/NIRCam imaging has enabled efficient selection of a large number of galaxy candidates at very high redshifts. Accurate and robust selection is crucial for statistical analyses, such as measurement of the ultraviolet luminosity function (UV LF). A variety of selection methods and criteria have been proposed in the literature. We evaluate the selection methodology of galaxy candidates at z≥8.5 using 183 candidates with secure spectroscopic redshifts obtained from the CAPERS JWST/NIRSpec PRISM program. The success of high-z confirmation is strongly correlated with the photometric signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) in the detection filter, the strength of the Lyman-α break, and the Δχ^2 between the best-fitting low-redshift and high-redshift spectral energy distribution model. However, the most efficient selection method is the use of a UV LF prior on the inferred p(z) of a source, raising the success rate from ≃ 35&percnt; to ≃ 77&percnt; while achieving greater completeness than conservative SNR and Δχ^2 cuts. Out of the 183 candidates we spectroscopically confirm 48 sources at z≥8.5 via detections of emission lines and/or the Lyman-α break (particularly important for those with no emission line detection). We report the highest-redshift source in CAPERS as UDS-111893 at z<sub>spec</sub>=12.98, with no sources confirmed at z&gt;13. This work provides spectroscopic quantification of JWST/NIRCam photometric selection at z≥8.5, and demonstrates the effectiveness of a UV LF prior for high-redshift photometric selection.
</p>

### 154) [2610.12121](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12121v1): Too many or too bright? Status and perspectives in the study of the UV Luminosity Function at z&gt;10

<p> A. Fontana, H. Atek, V. Bromm, A. J. Bunker, M. Castellano, P. Dayal, F. D'Eugenio, D. Elbaz, A. Ferrara, S. L. Finkelstein, S. Fujimoto, M. Giavalisco, Y. Harikane, C. Kobayashi, F. Pacucci, P. G. Pérez-González, P. Rosati, A. Trinca, R. Tripodi, P. Santini, E. Vanzella, A. Venditti, A. Weibel </p>
<p>
The finding that galaxies in the first 500Myr were much more numerous and/or brighter than expected is often cited as one of the main discoveries of the James Webb Space Telescope in its first four years of operation. This result is now solidly based on an impressive number of surveys that have detected, spectroscopically confirmed, and characterized a relatively large sample of galaxies up to z=14.44, the redshift of the most distant galaxy currently known. This finding has motivated the introduction of additional physical mechanisms into theoretical models to reproduce the observed numbers and luminosities. These results contrast with the failure, despite ongoing efforts, to confirm any galaxy candidate beyond this redshift. This review summarizes the status of the search for and characterization of galaxies at z&gt;9, focusing on the evolution of the UV Luminosity Function (UVLF), which is the main statistical description of the galaxy population that can be obtained at these redshifts. We first provide a comprehensive overview of the full set of JWST data obtained so far. We perform a 'meta&ndash;analysis' on the entire data set and provide a simple yet effective description of the global evolution of the UVLF, averaging over the various surveys. We also compare these results extensively with theoretical models that were developed to match the new JWST observations and that explore different physical mechanisms able to drive the observed evolution. We conclude with a summary of the contrasting results regarding the search for galaxy candidates at z&gt;15, and outline the requirements for future surveys aimed at breaking this barrier.
</p>

### 155) [2610.12100](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12100v1): A New Era for Supernova Science with the James Webb Space Telescope

<p> Justin Pierel, David Coulter, Christa DeCoursey, James DerKacy, Ori Fox, Melissa Shahbandeh, Matthew Siebert, Michael Engesser, Conor Larison, Thomas Moore, Armin Rest, Rodrigo Angulo, Michael Dulude, Eiichi Egami, Massimo Griggio, Mitchell Karmen, Xiaolong Li, Estefania Padilla Gonzalez, Koji Shukawa, Louis-Gregory Strolger </p>
<p>
Supernovae (SNe) are excellent probes of the physical, chemical, and cosmological context of galaxies across the history of the Universe. The James Webb Space Telescope (JWST ) has a truly unique combination of wavelength, sensitivity, and resolution, providing a new window into SN physics, progenitors, dust production, redshift evolving stellar populations, and cosmology since its launch. Here we summarize the astounding observations JWST has made of SNe in its first few years of operations, as well as the corresponding advancements in our understanding of the Universe. This includes detailed views of nearby SNe, the first spectroscopic samples of high-z SNe, and a significant number of gravitationally lensed SNe valuable for both cosmological inference and high signal-to-noise (S/N) spectroscopy. While the upcoming Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope is expected to revolutionize space-based time-domain astronomy, JWST and its unique capabilities will continue to play a complementary role by providing high-precision spectroscopic and photometric data for a broad range of SN science cases.
</p>

### 156) [2610.12055](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12055v1): Binding Energies of Astrophysically Relevant Molecules on CO<sub>2</sub> Clusters: Benchmarking and the Donor-Acceptor Origin of Surface-Dependent Binding

<p> Aneesa Ahmad, Catherine Walsh, Stefan Vogt-Geisse </p>
<p>
Carbon dioxide (CO<sub>2</sub>) is among the most abundant interstellar ices and has been observed by JWST in water-rich mixtures and pure CO<sub>2</sub> environments. Astrochemical models adopt binding energies (BEs) measured on water ice because few BEs are available for CO<sub>2</sub> ice. We calculate BE distributions for twelve adsorbates (N<sub>2</sub>, CH_4, HCl, C_2H<sub>2</sub>, H<sub>2</sub>S, HF, HCN, H<sub>2</sub>CO, HCOOH, HNC, CH_3OH, H<sub>2</sub>O) on CO<sub>2</sub> clusters containing two to five molecules using DFT calibrated against CCSD(T)/CBS, a coupled-cluster treatment extrapolated to the complete-basis-set limit. Binding strength depends on whether the adsorbate requires the surface to donate a hydrogen bond (H-bond). CO<sub>2</sub> can accept an H-bond but cannot donate one, and all six species compared with water ice from Ahmad et al. (2026) bind more weakly on CO<sub>2</sub>. The ratio of the BE on CO<sub>2</sub> to water ice ranges from 0.66 for H<sub>2</sub>CO and 0.75 for H<sub>2</sub>O to 0.87 for CH_3OH, 0.89 for HCOOH and 0.92 for C_2H<sub>2</sub>. Formaldehyde is predicted to desorb near 57 K from CO<sub>2</sub>-rich ice, compared with 85 K from water-rich ice, and could contribute to cold gas-phase H<sub>2</sub>CO. Although the mean binding energies are changing with cluster size for several species, the differences can be traced to local binding interactions likely to remain important in larger ice models. The BEs vary by more than 500 K between sites, while the desorption peaks range from 11 K for CH_4 to 132 K for HCOOH. The limited H-bonding capacity of CO<sub>2</sub> may favour separation of mixed H<sub>2</sub>O:CO<sub>2</sub> ice into CO<sub>2</sub>-rich domains.
</p>

### 157) [2610.10522](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10522v4): Widespread steep X-ray spectra of luminous z=5.85-7.64 quasars and their implications for future X-ray surveys

<p> A. Tortosa, L. Zappacosta, E. Piconcelli, I. Saccheo, G. Lanzuisi, E. Bertola, F. Vito, M. Bischetti, M. Brazzini, M. Castellano, S. Carniani, A. Comastri, S. Gallerani, R. Gilli, F. La Franca, P. Madau, R. Maiolino, G. Miniutti, B. Musiimenta, C. Piscitelli, F. Salvestrini, F. Tombesi, R. Tripodi, C. Vignali, M. Volonteri, A. Bongiorno, L. Borrelli, M. Brusa, I. V. Chilingaria, F. Civano, S. Cristiani, V. D'Odorico, D. De Cicco, C. Done, M. Elvis, X. Fan, C. Feruglio, F. Fiore, A. Grazian, M. Guainazzi, F. Haardt, A. Luminari, N. Menci, R. Middei, F. Nicastro, M. Paolillo, M. Perna, S. Puccetti, R. Schneider, V. Testa, R. Valiante, L. Vallini, E. Vanzella, G. Vietri </p>
<p>
Luminous quasars (QSOs) at the Epoch of Reionization (EoR, z≳6) host billion-solar-mass black holes assembled within the first Gyr of cosmic time. Their X-ray emission probes the innermost accreting regions and constrains the physical state of the corona during early supermassive black hole (SMBH) growth. Previous results from the HYPerluminous quasars at the Epoch of ReionizatION (HYPERION) sample of z ≳ 6 QSOs, powered by the most rapidly accreted SMBHs, suggested a link between X-ray spectral slope and long-term growth efficiency. We test whether the steep X-ray spectra of luminous z&gt;6 QSOs are a general property of early SMBH growth or a peculiarity of the most efficiently grown SMBHs, using a sample extended mainly to the lowest growth efficiencies. We analysed the largest and most X-ray sensitive sample of 28 luminous QSOs at z = 5.85-7.64 (26 used in the correlation analysis), combining HYPERION targets, luminous non-HYPERION sources, and QSOs powered by moderately accreting SMBHs newly observed in our XMM-Newton Large Programme. We re-derived black hole masses and growth indicators for the entire sample homogeneously, incorporating the latest JWST-based spectroscopic measurements to ensure the most consistent and high-quality characterization across all targets. We found no statistically significant correlation between coronal spectral parameters and BH growth indicators: luminous z ≳ 6 QSOs systematically show steep X-ray continua, with high photon indices and/or low high-energy cutoffs, consistent with cold coronae, possibly linked to rapid accretion. Finally, if steep X-ray slopes are common among early AGN, they may significantly reduce count rates, at fixed intrinsic luminosity, in future surveys of the EoR, affecting the detectability of steep-spectrum X-ray sources and thus the census of the early AGN population.
</p>

### 158) [2610.10117](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10117v1): MUSE-ALMA Haloes XVI: a census of the condensed baryons in z~0.5 HI absorption-selected galaxies

<p> T. O'Beirne, C. Péroux, M. Zwaan, C. Barfety, V. Bollo, J. Chen, A. L. Danhaive, N. M. Förster Schreiber, L. Tacconi, S. Weng, V. Kulkarni, L. Chemin, R. Augustin, B. Casavecchia, M. E. Cluver, M. Halley, A. Karki, V. A. Kilborn, O. I. Wong </p>
<p>
We use the MUSE-ALMA Haloes sample to build a census of the condensed baryons (stars and cold gas) to study the multiphase interstellar medium and circumgalactic medium (CGM). The sample includes 79 gas-rich galaxies at 0.2&lt;z&lt;1.2 located near strong HI Ly-alpha absorbers along quasar sightlines within projected separations of ~5-270 kpc and radial velocity separations of up to 500 km/s. HST spectroscopy characterises the absorbers, while HST imaging, MUSE and ALMA observations trace the stars, ionised gas and molecular gas. We find the following results. (i) HI masses inferred from optical properties show broad agreement with those derived from the CGM absorber properties for impact parameters &lt;100 kpc, although group environments can significantly influence these estimates. (ii) The molecular gas fraction declines more steeply with stellar mass than the HI fraction. This suggests that stars build up more rapidly than the cold gas reservoir, while massive galaxies either consume their molecular gas more efficiently or convert HI into H2 less efficiently. (iii) Galaxies with high stellar surface densities exhibit the highest baryon fractions. This may reflect baryonic feedback, which can produce flatter inner dark matter profiles in high surface density systems, or aperture effects, where the ionised gas kinematic tracer probes only the innermost, baryon-dominated regions in compact sources, but extends further into the dark matter halo in sources with extended baryonic distributions. (iv) A few galaxies are consistent with being the baryon-dominated descendants of the low mass z~6 galaxies detected by JWST, while many appear to be substantially more dark matter dominated, lower mass systems with progenitors beyond JWST detection limits. This study provides a multiscale, multiphase view of the gas in and around galaxies, allowing us to probe the baryon budget across cosmic time.
</p>
